     common radial-jet service.\n\nSelected derivative service: **9-point local polynomial, degree 8**,
     evaluated directly on the nonuniform ‘r=x‘ grid. The service was accepted only after regression against
     the archived exact Zhang-Kase representative.\n\n- ‘v12 = -v6/(2h)‘: max absolute residual **{q(chosen.
     v12_abs_residual.max())}**.\n- ‘v13‘: median scaled relative residual **{q(chosen.v13_rel_scaled.median())
     }**, 95% **{q(chosen.v13_rel_scaled.quantile(.95))}**, max **{q(chosen.v13_rel_scaled.max())}**.\n- ‘e4‘
     radial-jet isolation (archived ‘v13‘): median **{q(chosen.e4_isolated_rel_scaled.median())}**, 95% **{q(
     chosen.e4_isolated_rel_scaled.quantile(.95))}**, max **{q(chosen.e4_isolated_rel_scaled.max())}**.\n- ‘e4‘
      end-to-end (reconstructed ‘v13‘): median **{q(chosen.e4_rel_scaled.median())}**, 95% **{q(chosen.
     e4_rel_scaled.quantile(.95))}**, max **{q(chosen.e4_rel_scaled.max())}**.\n\nAll four higher-jet
     regression gates pass. In particular, the former ‘e4‘ spline pathology is absent; no physical conclusion
     is drawn from any route that fails this regression.\n\n## Scope\nThis closes the sensitive central ‘v12/
     v13/e4‘ regeneration layer. It does **not** by itself close the global finite-‘ell‘ mass matrix. The
     next accepted step is to use the same derivative service on one smooth selected Horndeski control/action
     section, generate the corrected Maxwell-Horndeski ‘a5‘, and then regenerate both handovers before running
     the profile-aware reducer.\n’’’
    (B/’SSZ_P5_HIGHER_JET_CLOSURE_2026-09-16.md’).write_text(report,encoding=’utf-8’)
    print(gate.to_string(index=False))
    print(’\n’+report)

if __name__==’__main__’:
    main()




C.5      Holonomic a5 closure
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""SSZ P5 Maxwell--Horndeski a5 holonomic closure audit, 2026-09-16.

For A0’=0 the exact Appendix-A relations imply
  a2 = a1’ - P a1 + R,
  a5 = a2’ - a1’’,
where
  P = phi’’/phi’ + h’/(2h),
  R = r/phi’ (f’/f-h’/h) a4.
Eliminating the numerically dangerous second derivative gives
  a5 = -P’ a1 - P a1’ + R’.

The selected a1 is obtained by integrating the first-order a2 identity from one


                                                      65
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                          Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


endpoint. Hence a1,a2,a5 belong to one internally consistent coefficient
profile; a1 is not differentiated twice from a rounded CSV.
"""
from pathlib import Path
import math
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from scipy.interpolate import CubicSpline, UnivariateSpline
from scipy.integrate import solve_ivp

B=Path(’/mnt/data’)
STRONG=B/’ssz_p5_F2_horndeski_carrier_unreduced_39of41_CORRECTED_2026-09-14.csv’
CORE=B/’ssz_p5_F2_core_punctured_horndeski_unreduced_39of41_2026-09-14.csv’


def local_poly_deriv(x,y,order=1,window=9,degree=8):
    x=np.asarray(x,float); y=np.asarray(y,float); n=len(x); out=np.empty(n)
    half=window//2
    for i in range(n):
        lo=max(0,i-half); hi=min(n,lo+window); lo=max(0,hi-window)
        xx=x[lo:hi]; yy=y[lo:hi]; x0=x[i]
        sc=float(np.max(np.abs(xx-x0))) or 1.0
        z=(xx-x0)/sc
        co=np.polynomial.polynomial.polyfit(z,yy,min(degree,len(xx)-1))
        out[i]=math.factorial(order)*co[order]/sc**order
    return out


def select(df,a2_smoothing=0.0):
    r=df.x.to_numpy(float); f=df.f.to_numpy(float); h=df.h.to_numpy(float)
    ph=(df.phi_r if ’phi_r’ in df.columns else df.phiprime).to_numpy(float)
    a1in=df.a1.to_numpy(float); a2in=df.a2.to_numpy(float); a4=df.a4.to_numpy(float)
    fp=local_poly_deriv(r,f); hp=local_poly_deriv(r,h); phipp=local_poly_deriv(r,ph)
    P=phipp/ph+0.5*hp/h
    R=r/ph*(fp/f-hp/h)*a4
    Pp=local_poly_deriv(r,P); Rp=local_poly_deriv(r,R)
    a2sp=UnivariateSpline(r,a2in,k=5,s=a2_smoothing)
    Psp=CubicSpline(r,P); Rsp=CubicSpline(r,R)
    sol=solve_ivp(lambda x,y:a2sp(x)+Psp(x)*y[0]-Rsp(x),
                  (r[0],r[-1]),[a1in[0]],t_eval=r,
                  rtol=2e-11,atol=1e-13,max_step=(r[-1]-r[0])/2000)
    if not sol.success: raise RuntimeError(sol.message)
    a1=sol.y[0]; a2=a2sp(r); a1p=a2+P*a1-R
    a5=-Pp*a1-P*a1p+Rp
    return pd.DataFrame({’x’:r,’u’:df.u if ’u’ in df else 1/r,
                          ’a1_archive’:a1in,’a1_selected’:a1,
                          ’a2_archive’:a2in,’a2_selected’:a2,
                          ’a5_archive’:df.a5,’a5_selected’:a5,
                          ’P’:P,’R’:R})


def rel(a,b): return np.abs(np.asarray(a)-np.asarray(b))/np.maximum(1,np.abs(np.asarray(b)))

def main():
    strong=pd.read_csv(STRONG); core=pd.read_csv(CORE)
    s0=select(strong,0.0)
    sr=rel(s0.a5_selected,s0.a5_archive)
    c0=select(core,0.0); c8=select(core,1e-8)
    conv=rel(c8.a5_selected,c0.a5_selected)
    a1shift=rel(c8.a1_selected,c8.a1_archive)

    s0.to_csv(B/’ssz_p5_strongH_a5_holonomic_regression_2026-09-16.csv’,index=False)
    c8.to_csv(B/’ssz_p5_core_a5_holonomic_selected_2026-09-16.csv’,index=False)
    pd.DataFrame({’x’:c8.x,’u’:c8.u,’a5_unsmoothed_identity’:c0.a5_selected,
                  ’a5_selected’:c8.a5_selected,’rel_scaled_difference’:conv}).to_csv(
        B/’ssz_p5_core_a5_selection_convergence_2026-09-16.csv’,index=False)

    gates=pd.DataFrame([
      [’strong-H a5 identity regression’,np.median(sr)<1e-5 and np.max(sr)<1e-3,float(np.max(sr)),’median <1e
     -5; max <1e-3’],


                                                         66
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                          Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


      [’core a5 selection convergence’,np.median(conv)<1e-5 and np.max(conv)<5e-4,float(np.max(conv)),’median
     <1e-5; max <5e-4’],
      [’core selected a1 stays in archived control tube’,np.median(a1shift)<1e-5 and np.max(a1shift)<5e-4,float
     (np.max(a1shift)),’median <1e-5; max <5e-4’],
    ],columns=[’gate’,’passed’,’reported_value’,’criterion’])
    gates.to_csv(B/’ssz_p5_holonomic_a5_gate_status_2026-09-16.csv’,index=False)

    report=f’’’# SSZ P5 -- Holonomic Maxwell-Horndeski ‘a5‘ closure\n**Date:** 2026-09-16\n\nThe exact Maxwell-
     Horndeski Appendix-A identities were combined so that the selected pure-Horndeski ‘a5‘ no longer requires
     a second numerical differentiation of the archived ‘a1‘ column. For ‘A0’=0‘,\n\n‘‘‘math\na_2=a_1’-P a_1+R
     ,\qquad a_5=-P’a_1-Pa_1’+R’,\n‘‘‘\nwith ‘P=phi’’/phi’+h’/(2h)‘ and ‘R=r/phi’(f’/f-h’/h)a4‘.\n\n##
     Independent regression\nOn the smooth strong-H carrier the regenerated ‘a5‘ has median scaled relative
     residual **{np.median(sr):.6e}**, 95% **{np.quantile(sr,.95):.6e}**, and maximum **{np.max(sr):.6e}**.\n\n
     ## Punctured-core selected member\nThe old pointwise core ‘a5‘ column is derivative-noise contaminated. A
      deterministic selected member was therefore generated from the first-order ‘a2‘ identity. Comparing zero
      smoothing with the very small ‘s=1e-8‘ ‘a2‘ regularization gives median ‘a5‘ change **{np.median(conv):.6
     e}**, 95% **{np.quantile(conv,.95):.6e}**, and maximum **{np.max(conv):.6e}**. The corresponding selected
      ‘a1‘ remains within a maximum scaled distance **{np.max(a1shift):.6e}** of the archived full-rank control
      target.\n\nAll three gates pass. This fixes the old missing-‘-a1’’‘ implementation error without
     inventing a physical instability or differentiating a rounded coefficient table twice.\n’’’
    (B/’SSZ_P5_HOLONOMIC_A5_CLOSURE_2026-09-16.md’).write_text(report,encoding=’utf-8’)
    print(gates.to_string(index=False)); print(’\n’+report)

C.6      Full algebraic constraint maps
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
SSZ P5 full algebraic constraint-map audit (JET9D8), 2026-09-16.

Implements the exact manuscript Sec. 20.4 constraint architecture after the
generalized-psi H0 reduction, using the accepted local polynomial radial jet
service rather than CubicSpline differentiation.

Physical basis: y = (psi, dphi, V), with y_r = (psi’, dphi’, V’).
Maps exported:
  h1 = H10*y + H11*y_r
  H2 = H20*y + H21*y_r
  H1 = T10*dot(y) + T11*dot(y_r)
  dA1 = A10*dot(y) + A11*dot(y_r)
  dA0 = A00*y + A01*y_r
"""
from pathlib import Path
import importlib.util
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

B = Path(’/mnt/data’)
SLOTS = [*[f’a{i}’ for i in range(1,10)],
         *[f’b{i}’ for i in range(1,6)],
         *[f’c{i}’ for i in range(1,7)],
         *[f’d{i}’ for i in range(1,5)],
         *[f’e{i}’ for i in range(1,5)],
         *[f’v{i}’ for i in range(1,14)]]

spec=importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(’hj’,B/’ssz_p5_higher_jet_closure_2026-09-16.py’)
hj=importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec); spec.loader.exec_module(hj)

def dr(r,y,window=9,degree=8):
    return hj.local_poly_deriv(np.asarray(r,float),np.asarray(y,float),1,window,degree)

def maps(df,L,window=9,degree=8):
    d=df.copy().reset_index(drop=True)
    miss=[k for k in [’x’]+SLOTS if k not in d.columns]
    if miss: raise KeyError(miss)
    r=d.x.to_numpy(float); n=len(d)


                                                      67
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                             Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


    q={k:d[k].to_numpy(float) for k in SLOTS}

    p=q[’a1’]/q[’a3’]
    qq=L*q[’a4’]/q[’a3’]
    pp=dr(r,p,window,degree); qrp=dr(r,qq,window,degree)

    Braw=q[’a2’]-q[’v2’]*q[’v4’]/(2*q[’v1’])
    Cphi=q[’a5’]+L*q[’a6’]-q[’v2’]*q[’v5’]/(2*q[’v1’])
    CH2=q[’a7’]+L*q[’a8’]-q[’v2’]*q[’v3’]/(2*q[’v1’])
    Ch1=L*(q[’a9’]-q[’v2’]*q[’v6’]/(2*q[’v1’]))
    Beff=Braw-q[’a3’]*pp-CH2*p
    Dh1=Ch1-q[’a3’]*qrp-CH2*qq

    # h1 = H10 y + H11 y’
    H10=np.zeros((n,3)); H11=np.zeros((n,3))
    H10[:,0]=-CH2/Dh1
    H10[:,1]=-Cphi/Dh1
    H10[:,2]=-q[’v2’]/Dh1
    H11[:,0]=-q[’a3’]/Dh1
    H11[:,1]=-Beff/Dh1

    # H2 = psi - q h1 - p dphi’
    H20=np.zeros((n,3)); H21=np.zeros((n,3))
    H20[:,0]=1.0
    H20-=qq[:,None]*H10
    H21-=qq[:,None]*H11
    H21[:,1]-=p

    # J1 = b3 dot dphi + b4 dot H2 + L b5 dot h1.
    J10=np.zeros((n,3)); J11=np.zeros((n,3))
    J10[:,1]+=q[’b3’]
    J10+=q[’b4’][:,None]*H20 + (L*q[’b5’])[:,None]*H10
    J11+=q[’b4’][:,None]*H21 + (L*q[’b5’])[:,None]*H11

    # R1=-J1/L ; R2=-(2v1/L)dot V +(v6/2)dot h1.
    R10=-J10/L; R11=-J11/L
    R20=(q[’v6’]/2)[:,None]*H10
    R21=(q[’v6’]/2)[:,None]*H11
    R20[:,2]+=-2*q[’v1’]/L

    Delta=4*q[’b1’]*q[’v10’]-q[’v11’]**2

    # H1=(2v10 R1-v11 R2)/Delta
    T10=((2*q[’v10’])[:,None]*R10-q[’v11’][:,None]*R20)/Delta[:,None]
    T11=((2*q[’v10’])[:,None]*R11-q[’v11’][:,None]*R21)/Delta[:,None]

    # dA1=(-v11 R1+2b1 R2)/Delta
    A10=(-q[’v11’][:,None]*R10+(2*q[’b1’])[:,None]*R20)/Delta[:,None]
    A11=(-q[’v11’][:,None]*R11+(2*q[’b1’])[:,None]*R21)/Delta[:,None]

    # dA0=(v1 V)’/(L v9) -(v8 h1+v12 H2+v13 dphi)/(2v9)
    v1p=dr(r,q[’v1’],window,degree)
    A00=-(q[’v8’][:,None]*H10+q[’v12’][:,None]*H20)/(2*q[’v9’])[:,None]
    A01=-(q[’v8’][:,None]*H11+q[’v12’][:,None]*H21)/(2*q[’v9’])[:,None]
    A00[:,1]+=-q[’v13’]/(2*q[’v9’])
    A00[:,2]+=v1p/(L*q[’v9’])
    A01[:,2]+=q[’v1’]/(L*q[’v9’])

    return dict(
        p=p,q=qq,Dh1=Dh1,DeltaV=Delta,pivotA0=2*L*q[’v9’],
        H10=H10,H11=H11,H20=H20,H21=H21,
        H1dot0=T10,H1dot1=T11,dA1dot0=A10,dA1dot1=A11,
        dA00=A00,dA01=A01,
        cancel_dphi2=q[’a1’]-q[’a3’]*p,
        cancel_h1prime=L*q[’a4’]-q[’a3’]*qq
    )

def audit(df,label,Ls=(6,12,20,30,42,72,110,210,420,1000)):
    rows=[]
    for L in Ls:


                                                         68
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                            Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


        m=maps(df,float(L))
        row={
          ’sector’:label,’L’:L,
          ’min_abs_Dh1’:float(np.min(np.abs(m[’Dh1’]))),
          ’min_abs_DeltaV’:float(np.min(np.abs(m[’DeltaV’]))),
          ’min_abs_2Lv9’:float(np.min(np.abs(m[’pivotA0’]))),
          ’max_abs_dphi2_cancel’:float(np.max(np.abs(m[’cancel_dphi2’]))),
          ’max_abs_h1prime_cancel’:float(np.max(np.abs(m[’cancel_h1prime’]))),
          ’max_abs_h1_map’:float(max(np.max(np.abs(m[’H10’])),np.max(np.abs(m[’H11’])))),
          ’max_abs_H1_map’:float(max(np.max(np.abs(m[’H1dot0’])),np.max(np.abs(m[’H1dot1’])))),
          ’max_abs_dA1_map’:float(max(np.max(np.abs(m[’dA1dot0’])),np.max(np.abs(m[’dA1dot1’])))),
          ’max_abs_dA0_map’:float(max(np.max(np.abs(m[’dA00’])),np.max(np.abs(m[’dA01’])))),
        }
        rows.append(row)
    return pd.DataFrame(rows)

if __name__==’__main__’:
    central=pd.read_csv(B/’ssz_p5_integrable_svt_unreduced_even_coefficients_2026-09-12.csv’)

C.7      Profile-aware operator reducer
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Profile-aware SSZ P5 even-parity differential-operator reducer.

Builds the reduced Euler-Lagrange differential operator directly from the
unreduced 41-slot Zhang-Kase action after the accepted generalized-psi
constraint maps. All radial product rules are retained with JET9D8.

This is an audit/reconstruction layer, not by itself a global QNM claim.
"""
from __future__ import annotations
from pathlib import Path
from math import comb
import importlib.util
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

B=Path(’/mnt/data’)

# accepted derivative service
spec=importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(’hj’,B/’ssz_p5_higher_jet_closure_2026-09-16.py’)
hj=importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec); spec.loader.exec_module(hj)
# accepted full algebraic maps (uploaded name may carry (1))
FM=B/’ssz_hybrid_full_constraint_maps_JET9D8(1).py’
if not FM.exists(): FM=B/’ssz_hybrid_full_constraint_maps_JET9D8.py’
spec=importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(’fm’,FM)
fm=importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec); spec.loader.exec_module(fm)

SLOTS=[*[f’a{i}’ for i in range(1,10)],*[f’b{i}’ for i in range(1,6)],
       *[f’c{i}’ for i in range(1,7)],*[f’d{i}’ for i in range(1,5)],
       *[f’e{i}’ for i in range(1,5)],*[f’v{i}’ for i in range(1,14)]]


_JET_CACHE={}
def _jet_weights(r,order,window=9,degree=8):
    r=np.asarray(r,float)
    key=(r.shape, r.tobytes(), int(order), int(window), int(degree))
    if key in _JET_CACHE: return _JET_CACHE[key]
    n=len(r); half=window//2
    inds=np.empty((n,window),dtype=int); weights=np.zeros((n,window),float)
    import math
    for i in range(n):


                                                      69
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                          Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


        lo=max(0,i-half); hi=min(n,lo+window); lo=max(0,hi-window)
        idx=np.arange(lo,hi,dtype=int); xx=r[idx]; x0=r[i]
        scale=float(np.max(np.abs(xx-x0))) or 1.0
        z=(xx-x0)/scale
        deg=min(degree,len(idx)-1)
        V=np.polynomial.polynomial.polyvander(z,deg)
        # coef = solve(V,y), hence derivative weights are row(order) of inv(V)
        if order<=deg:
            inv=np.linalg.inv(V)
            w=math.factorial(order)*inv[order,:]/scale**order
        else:
            w=np.zeros(len(idx))
        inds[i,:len(idx)]=idx
        if len(idx)<window: inds[i,len(idx):]=idx[-1]
        weights[i,:len(idx)]=w
    _JET_CACHE[key]=(inds,weights)
    return inds,weights

def deriv(r,a,order,window=9,degree=8):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    if order==0: return a
    inds,w=_jet_weights(r,order,window,degree)
    vals=a[inds] # n,window,...
    return np.einsum(’nw,nw...->n...’,w,vals,optimize=True)


def addop(dst,key,val):
    if key in dst: dst[key] += val
    else: dst[key] = np.array(val,float,copy=True)


def field_maps(df,L,window=9,degree=8):
    """Return field -> scalar row differential operator on y=(psi,dphi,V)."""
    m=fm.maps(df,L,window,degree)
    n=len(df); z=np.zeros((n,3)); ephi=z.copy(); ephi[:,1]=1.; eV=z.copy(); eV[:,2]=1.
    return {
      ’dphi’: {(0,0):ephi},
      ’V’: {(0,0):eV},
      ’h1’: {(0,0):m[’H10’],(0,1):m[’H11’]},
      ’H2’: {(0,0):m[’H20’],(0,1):m[’H21’]},
      ’H1’: {(1,0):m[’H1dot0’],(1,1):m[’H1dot1’]},
      ’dA1’:{(1,0):m[’dA1dot0’],(1,1):m[’dA1dot1’]},
      ’dA0’:{(0,0):m[’dA00’],(0,1):m[’dA01’]},
    },m


def apply_derivative(op,dt,dr,r,window=9,degree=8):
    """Apply D_t^dt D_r^dr to a scalar-row differential operator."""
    out={}
    for (pt,pr),a in op.items():
        for k in range(dr+1):
            ak=deriv(r,a,k,window,degree) if k else a
            key=(pt+dt, pr+dr-k)
            addop(out,key,comb(dr,k)*ak)
    return out


def action_terms(df,L):
    """Exact non-H0 terms of the common unreduced even action.

    Every tuple is (coefficient array, field A, deriv A, field B, deriv B).
    H0-proportional terms are absent because the generalized-psi map enforces
    the H0 multiplier constraint identically; the H0^2 coefficient is audited
    separately before this reduction is accepted.
    """
    q={k:df[k].to_numpy(float) for k in SLOTS}; n=len(df)
    T=[]
    def add(c,A,da,B,db): T.append((np.asarray(c,float),A,da,B,db))
    # metric/scalar non-H0 block
    add(L*q[’b1’],’H1’,(0,0),’H1’,(0,0))


                                                      70
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                          Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


    add(q[’b2’],’H1’,(0,0),’dphi’,(1,1))
    add(q[’b3’],’H1’,(0,0),’dphi’,(1,0))
    add(q[’b4’],’H1’,(0,0),’H2’,(1,0))
    add(L*q[’b5’],’H1’,(0,0),’h1’,(1,0))
    add(q[’c1’],’dphi’,(1,0),’H2’,(1,0))
    add(q[’c2’],’H2’,(0,0),’dphi’,(0,1))
    add(q[’c3’]+L*q[’c4’],’H2’,(0,0),’dphi’,(0,0))
    add(L*q[’c5’],’H2’,(0,0),’h1’,(0,0))
    add(q[’c6’],’H2’,(0,0),’H2’,(0,0))
    add(L*q[’d1’],’h1’,(1,0),’h1’,(1,0))
    add(L*q[’d2’],’h1’,(0,0),’dphi’,(0,1))
    add(L*q[’d3’],’h1’,(0,0),’dphi’,(0,0))
    add(L*q[’d4’],’h1’,(0,0),’h1’,(0,0))
    add(q[’e1’],’dphi’,(1,0),’dphi’,(1,0))
    add(q[’e2’],’dphi’,(0,1),’dphi’,(0,1))
    add(q[’e3’]+L*q[’e4’],’dphi’,(0,0),’dphi’,(0,0))

    # auxiliary-vector form, H0 set to zero after its exact constraint
    add(2*q[’v1’],’V’,(0,0),’dA0’,(0,1))
    add(-2*q[’v1’],’V’,(0,0),’dA1’,(1,0))
    add(-q[’v1’],’V’,(0,0),’V’,(0,0))
    # V*S0 and -S0^2/(4 v1), S0 excludes v2 H0
    parts=[(’H2’,q[’v3’]),(’dphi_r’,q[’v4’]),(’dphi’,q[’v5’]),(’h1’,L*q[’v6’])]
    pmap={’H2’:(’H2’,(0,0)),’dphi_r’:(’dphi’,(0,1)),’dphi’:(’dphi’,(0,0)),’h1’:(’h1’,(0,0))}
    for name,c in parts:
        f,dd=pmap[name]; add(c,’V’,(0,0),f,dd)
    for ia,(na,ca) in enumerate(parts):
        fa,da=pmap[na]
        for ib in range(ia,len(parts)):
             nb,cb=parts[ib]; fb,db=pmap[nb]
             fac=2.0 if ib!=ia else 1.0
             add(-fac*ca*cb/(4*q[’v1’]),fa,da,fb,db)
    add(0.5*L*q[’v6’],’h1’,(0,0),’dA1’,(1,0))
    add(L*q[’v8’],’h1’,(0,0),’dA0’,(0,0))
    add(L*q[’v9’],’dA0’,(0,0),’dA0’,(0,0))
    add(L*q[’v10’],’dA1’,(0,0),’dA1’,(0,0))
    add(L*q[’v11’],’H1’,(0,0),’dA1’,(0,0))
    add(L*q[’v12’],’H2’,(0,0),’dA0’,(0,0))
    add(L*q[’v13’],’dphi’,(0,0),’dA0’,(0,0))
    return T


def adjoint_product(A,c,Bop,r,window=9,degree=8):
    """A^dagger * c * B, for scalar-row operators A,B; returns 3x3 operator."""
    n=len(r); out={}
    for (pt,pr),av in A.items():
      for (qt,qr),bv in Bop.items():
        base=av[:,:,None]*bv[:,None,:]*c[:,None,None] # output i, input j
        sgn=(-1.0)**(pt+pr)
        for k in range(pr+1):
            bk=deriv(r,base,k,window,degree) if k else base
            key=(pt+qt, pr-k+qr)
            addop(out,key,sgn*comb(pr,k)*bk)
    return out


def reduced_operator(df,L,window=9,degree=8):
    d=df.copy().reset_index(drop=True)
    # accepted central Appendix-A branch
    d[’v12’]=-d[’v6’]/(2*d[’h’])
    r=d.x.to_numpy(float)
    F,m=field_maps(d,L,window,degree)
    P={}
    for c,A,da,Bn,db in action_terms(d,L):
         Aop=apply_derivative(F[A],da[0],da[1],r,window,degree)
         Bop=apply_derivative(F[Bn],db[0],db[1],r,window,degree)
         X=adjoint_product(Aop,c,Bop,r,window,degree)
         Y=adjoint_product(Bop,c,Aop,r,window,degree)
         for k,v in X.items(): addop(P,k,v)
         for k,v in Y.items(): addop(P,k,v)


                                                       71
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                          Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


    # h0 quadratic identity (should vanish before setting H0=0)
    h0_quad=d[’v7’].to_numpy(float)-d[’v2’].to_numpy(float)**2/(4*d[’v1’].to_numpy(float))
    return P,m,h0_quad


def canonical_audit(df,L,window=9,degree=8):
    P,m,h0q=reduced_operator(df,L,window,degree)
    r=df.x.to_numpy(float); n=len(df)
    Z=lambda: np.zeros((n,3,3))
    get=lambda key: P.get(key,Z())
    # Euler-operator principal blocks. With target action normalization,
    # K=-P20/2, G=P02/2 if source action is written without overall 1/2.
    # Both raw P and half-normalized blocks are exported for regression.
    P20=get((2,0)); P11=get((1,1)); P10=get((1,0)); P02=get((0,2)); P01=get((0,1)); P00=get((0,0))
    K=-0.5*P20; G=0.5*P02; R=-0.5*P11
    # Zhang--Kase Eq. (4.25) contains an additional antisymmetric radial
    # connection block S: Y’^T S Y, S^T=-S. In the project sign convention
    # we keep -Y’^T G Y’ but retain +Y’^T S Y. For the Euler operator,
    #   P01 = P02’ - 2 S,   P00 = -2 M - S’
    # where M below is the project convention (minus sign in the action).
    P11p=np.empty_like(P11); P02p=np.empty_like(P02)
    for i in range(3):
      for j in range(3):
        P11p[:,i,j]=deriv(r,P11[:,i,j],1,window,degree)
        P02p[:,i,j]=deriv(r,P02[:,i,j],1,window,degree)
    S=0.5*(P02p-P01)
    Sp=np.empty_like(S)
    for i in range(3):
      for j in range(3):
        Sp[:,i,j]=deriv(r,S[:,i,j],1,window,degree)
    M=-0.5*(P00+Sp)
    diagnostics={
      ’max_h0_quadratic’:float(np.max(np.abs(h0q))),
      ’max_P20_asym’:float(np.max(np.abs(P20-np.swapaxes(P20,1,2)))),
      ’max_P02_asym’:float(np.max(np.abs(P02-np.swapaxes(P02,1,2)))),
      ’max_P11_asym’:float(np.max(np.abs(P11-np.swapaxes(P11,1,2)))),
      ’max_time_first_sym_res’:float(np.max(np.abs(0.5*(P10+np.swapaxes(P10,1,2))-0.5*P11p))),
      ’max_radial_first_sym_res’:float(np.max(np.abs(0.5*(P01+np.swapaxes(P01,1,2))-P02p))),
    }
    high=[k for k in P if k[0]>2 or k[1]>2]
    diagnostics[’higher_operator_keys’]=str(sorted(high))
    diagnostics[’max_high_order’]=float(max([np.max(np.abs(P[k])) for k in high],default=0.0))
    diagnostics[’max_S_sym’]=float(np.max(np.abs(S+np.swapaxes(S,1,2))))
    diagnostics[’max_R_abs’]=float(np.max(np.abs(R)))
    return dict(P=P,K=K,R=R,G=G,S=S,M=M,maps=m,diagnostics=diagnostics)


def prepare_central():
    d=pd.read_csv(B/’ssz_p5_integrable_svt_unreduced_even_coefficients_2026-09-12.csv’).copy()
    ce=pd.read_csv(B/’ssz_p5_F2b_central_c3_e3_SELECTED_REPRESENTATIVE_2026-09-15.csv’)
    d[’c3’]=ce.c3_selected.to_numpy(float); d[’e3’]=ce.e3_selected.to_numpy(float)
    d[’v12’]=-d[’v6’]/(2*d[’h’])
    return d

if __name__==’__main__’:
    d=prepare_central(); rows=[]
    for L in (6,12,20,42,110,420,1000):
        a=canonical_audit(d,float(L)); row={’L’:L,**a[’diagnostics’]}
        for nm in (’K’,’G’,’S’,’M’):
            A=a[nm]; row[f’{nm}_maxabs’]=float(np.max(np.abs(A)))
        rows.append(row)
    out=pd.DataFrame(rows)
    out.to_csv(B/’ssz_p5_profile_operator_structural_audit_2026-09-16.csv’,index=False)
    print(out.to_string(index=False))




C.8      High-L mass closure


                                                      72
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                          Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""SSZ P5 F2b: action-derived high-L mass closure audit.

Derives/tests the high-L central even-parity mass entries directly against the
unreduced Zhang--Kase action architecture after the exact H0/psi reduction.

Key point: M12 contains TWO different m1-like combinations. The c4 sector
uses the printed m1-plus combination, while the dA0/v9 Schur-complement sector
uses the reflected m1-minus combination. Collapsing them to one m1 is the
source of the previously persistent M12 mismatch.
"""
from pathlib import Path
import importlib.util
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from scipy.interpolate import CubicSpline

BASE = Path(’/mnt/data’)
COEFF = BASE/’ssz_p5_integrable_svt_unreduced_even_coefficients_2026-09-12.csv’
CE = BASE/’ssz_p5_F2b_central_c3_e3_SELECTED_REPRESENTATIVE_2026-09-15.csv’
REDUCER = BASE/’ssz_hybrid_jet_aware_constraint_reducer.py’

d = pd.read_csv(COEFF).copy()
ce = pd.read_csv(CE)
# c3,e3 are subleading in the high-L identities below; they are inserted only
# so the finite-L direct reducer can be evaluated as a convergence probe.
d[’c3’] = ce.c3_selected.to_numpy(float)
d[’e3’] = ce.e3_selected.to_numpy(float)

r = d.x.to_numpy(float)
u = d.u.to_numpy(float)
order = np.argsort(r)
rs = r[order]

def dr(y):
    y = np.asarray(y, float)
    sp = CubicSpline(rs, y[order])
    z = np.empty_like(y)
    z[order] = sp(rs, 1)
    return z

h = d.h.to_numpy(float)
Ap = d.A0prime.to_numpy(float)
phip = d.phiprime.to_numpy(float)
a4 = d.a4.to_numpy(float)
a6 = d.a6.to_numpy(float)
a9 = d.a9.to_numpy(float)
v1 = d.v1.to_numpy(float)
v4 = d.v4.to_numpy(float)
v5 = d.v5.to_numpy(float)
v6 = d.v6.to_numpy(float)
v8 = d.v8.to_numpy(float)
v9 = d.v9.to_numpy(float)
v13 = d.v13.to_numpy(float)
c4 = d.c4.to_numpy(float)
c5 = d.c5.to_numpy(float)
d2 = d.d2.to_numpy(float)
d3 = d.d3.to_numpy(float)
e4 = d.e4.to_numpy(float)

# Appendix-A identities used by the algebraic high-L reduction.
identity = pd.DataFrame([
    [’a3 + r a4’, np.max(np.abs(d.a3.to_numpy(float) + r*a4))],
    [’a8 + a4/(2h)’, np.max(np.abs(d.a8.to_numpy(float) + a4/(2*h)))],
    [’v2 - A0prime*v1’, np.max(np.abs(d.v2.to_numpy(float) - Ap*v1))],
    [’v3 + A0prime*v1 + phiprime*v4/2 + r*v6’,
     np.max(np.abs(d.v3.to_numpy(float) + Ap*v1 + .5*phip*v4 + r*v6))],
    [’v12 + v6/(2h)’, np.max(np.abs(d.v12.to_numpy(float) + v6/(2*h)))],
], columns=[’identity’,’max_abs_residual’])


                                                      73
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                          Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16



# Two genuinely different m1-like combinations generated by different action
# sectors. z1 is their common non-v8 piece.
z1 = (a4 + r*a9 - .5*r*Ap*v6)*v6
m1_plus = r*a4*v8 + z1        # printed Eq.(90), correct in the c4 sector
m1_minus = -r*a4*v8 + z1      # generated in the dA0/v9 Schur sector
m2 = 2*c5*v1 + (Ap*v1 + .5*phip*v4)*v6
m3_plus = a4*dr(v1) + .5*Ap*v1*v6
m4 = 2*Ap*v1 + phip*v4
m5_minus = a4*v13 - a6*v6

# Action-derived leading high-L mass entries.
M11 = -r**2*v6**2/(4*v1)
M13 = -r*v6/2
M33 = -v1
M22 = e4 + 2*h*c4*a6/a4 - m5_minus**2/(4*a4**2*v9)

T_d3 = -r*d3/2
T_c4 = -h*c4*z1/(a4*v6)
T_m2 = -r*h*a6*m2/(2*a4*v1)
T_v5 = r*v5*v6/(4*v1)
T_A0 = -m1_minus*m5_minus/(4*a4**2*v9)
IBP_inside = (2*r*h*c4
               + r*(2*d2*v1-v4*v6)/(2*v1)
               + r*v6*m5_minus/(2*a4*v9))
T_IBP = .25*dr(IBP_inside)
M12 = T_d3 + T_c4 + T_m2 + T_v5 + T_A0 + T_IBP

M23 = (h*Ap*c4*v1/a4 + v5/2
       + m3_plus*m5_minus/(2*a4**2*v9)
       - h*a6*m4/(2*a4)
       - .25*dr(v4 + v1*m5_minus/(a4*v9)))

# Direct finite-L reducer, preserving exactly the same unreduced action route
# used in the earlier central mass audit.
spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(’red’, REDUCER)
red = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
spec.loader.exec_module(red)

def direct_mass(L):
    h0 = red.generalized_psi_h0_reduction(d, L, r_col=’x’)
    D = h0.D_h1_pivot.to_numpy(float)
    p = h0.p_a1_over_a3.to_numpy(float)
    q = h0.q_La4_over_a3.to_numpy(float)
    h0v = np.c_[-h0.C_H2/D, -h0.C_phi/D, -d.v2/D]
    h1v = np.c_[-d.a3/D, -h0.B_eff/D, np.zeros(len(d))]
    ep = np.array([1.,0.,0.]); ef = np.array([0.,1.,0.]); ev = np.array([0.,0.,1.])
    H0 = np.empty_like(h0v); H1 = np.empty_like(h1v)
    for i in range(len(d)):
        H0[i] = ep - q[i]*h0v[i]
        H1[i] = -q[i]*h1v[i] - p[i]*ef
    Hv = np.concatenate([H0,H1], axis=1)
    hv = np.concatenate([h0v,h1v], axis=1)
    v1p = dr(v1)
    Cs = np.zeros((len(d),6,6))
    def add(C,coef,a,b=None):
        if b is None: b=a
        C += .5*coef*(np.outer(a,b)+np.outer(b,a))
    for i,c in d.iterrows():
        C = np.zeros((6,6)); Hvec = Hv[i]; hvec = hv[i]
        ephi = np.r_[ef,np.zeros(3)]; ephip = np.r_[np.zeros(3),ef]
        eV = np.r_[ev,np.zeros(3)]; eVp = np.r_[np.zeros(3),ev]
        add(C,float(c.c2),Hvec,ephip)
        add(C,float(c.c3+L*c.c4),Hvec,ephi)
        add(C,L*float(c.c5),Hvec,hvec)
        add(C,float(c.c6),Hvec,Hvec)
        add(C,L*float(c.d2),hvec,ephip)
        add(C,L*float(c.d3),hvec,ephi)
        add(C,L*float(c.d4),hvec,hvec)
        add(C,float(c.e2),ephip,ephip)


                                                      74
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                          Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


        add(C,float(c.e3+L*c.e4),ephi,ephi)
        S0 = float(c.v3)*Hvec + float(c.v4)*ephip + float(c.v5)*ephi + L*float(c.v6)*hvec
        add(C,-float(c.v1),eV,eV); add(C,1.,eV,S0); add(C,-1/(4*float(c.v1)),S0,S0)
        J0 = (-2*v1p[i]*eV - 2*float(c.v1)*eVp
              + L*(float(c.v8)*hvec + float(c.v12)*Hvec + float(c.v13)*ephi))
        add(C,-1/(4*L*float(c.v9)),J0,J0)
        Cs[i] = C
    A = Cs[:,:3,:3]
    B = Cs[:,:3,3:]
    Bs = .5*(B + np.swapaxes(B,1,2))
    Bp = np.empty_like(Bs)
    for ia in range(3):
        for ib in range(3):
            Bp[:,ia,ib] = dr(Bs[:,ia,ib])
    return A-Bp

trusted = (u >= 0.612) & (u <= 0.708)
def rel(a,b):
    return np.abs(a-b)/np.maximum(1.0,np.abs(b))

rows=[]
last=None
for L in [1e5,1e6,1e7,1e8,1e9]:
    M = direct_mass(float(L)); last=M
    pairs = {
        ’M11’: (M[:,0,0], M11),
        ’M12’: (M[:,0,1], M12),
        ’M13’: (M[:,0,2], M13),
        ’M22_over_L’: (M[:,1,1]/L, M22),
        ’M23’: (M[:,1,2], M23),
        ’M33’: (M[:,2,2], M33),
    }
    row={’L’:L}
    for name,(aa,bb) in pairs.items():
        er=rel(aa,bb)
        row[name+’_median_rel’]=np.median(er[trusted])
        row[name+’_max_rel_trusted’]=np.max(er[trusted])
    rows.append(row)
conv = pd.DataFrame(rows)

# Termwise pointwise audit at the largest L.
termwise = pd.DataFrame({
    ’u’:u,’x’:r,
    ’T_d3’:T_d3,’T_c4’:T_c4,’T_m2’:T_m2,’T_v5’:T_v5,
    ’T_A0_Schur’:T_A0,’T_IBP’:T_IBP,
    ’M12_action_formula’:M12,
    ’M12_direct_L1e9’:last[:,0,1],
    ’M12_abs_residual_L1e9’:np.abs(last[:,0,1]-M12),
    ’M12_rel_residual_L1e9’:rel(last[:,0,1],M12),
    ’m1_plus_c4_sector’:m1_plus,
    ’m1_minus_A0_sector’:m1_minus,
    ’m5_minus_action’:m5_minus,
    ’IBP_inside_action’:IBP_inside,
})

identity.to_csv(BASE/’ssz_p5_F2b_appendix_identity_residuals_2026-09-15.csv’, index=False)
conv.to_csv(BASE/’ssz_p5_F2b_action_highL_mass_convergence_2026-09-15.csv’, index=False)
termwise.to_csv(BASE/’ssz_p5_F2b_M12_termwise_action_audit_2026-09-15.csv’, index=False)

print(’Appendix identities:’)
print(identity.to_string(index=False))
print(’\nHigh-L convergence on 0.612 <= u <= 0.708:’)
print(conv.to_string(index=False))
print(’\nM12 L=1e9 median/max rel:’,
      np.median(termwise.loc[trusted,’M12_rel_residual_L1e9’]),
      np.max(termwise.loc[trusted,’M12_rel_residual_L1e9’]))

    for k,x in candidates.items():
      # compare project M and opposite sign, retain better for diagnosis
      eplus=rel(x,T[k]); eminus=rel(-x,T[k])
      row[k+’_proj_med’]=float(np.median(eplus[trusted])); row[k+’_proj_max’]=float(np.max(eplus[trusted]))

C.10       Common unreduced kernel
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Common unreduced even-parity kernel for SSZ Horndeski + U(1)-SVT.

This module encodes the shared gauge-fixed quadratic-action structure of
Kase & Tsujikawa (2023), Eqs. (4.12)-(4.14), and Zhang & Kase (2024),
Eqs. (4.12)-(4.14), *before* theory-specific constraint elimination.

Fields (common gauge):
    H0, H1, H2, h1, dphi, dA0, dA1, V
where V is the auxiliary vector variable introduced to linearize
(dA0’ - dot(dA1))^2.

The local symbol freezes radial background coefficients. This is the
appropriate eikonal/principal-symbol operation: radial derivatives of the


                                                      76
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                           Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


background coefficients are lower differential order.   The full finite-l
operator/QNM problem requires restoring them later.

Important: this module does NOT claim a final hybrid PASS. It supplies the
correct common operator architecture so Horndeski and genuine-SVT coefficient
contributions can be combined before the constraints are eliminated.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Dict, Mapping, Tuple, List
import numpy as np

FIELDS = ("H0", "H1", "H2", "h1", "dphi", "dA0", "dA1", "V")
FI = {name: i for i, name in enumerate(FIELDS)}

A_KEYS = tuple(f"a{i}" for i in range(1,10))
B_KEYS = tuple(f"b{i}" for i in range(1,6))
C_KEYS = tuple(f"c{i}" for i in range(1,7))
D_KEYS = tuple(f"d{i}" for i in range(1,5))
E_KEYS = tuple(f"e{i}" for i in range(1,5))
V_KEYS = tuple(f"v{i}" for i in range(1,14))
CANONICAL_KEYS = A_KEYS+B_KEYS+C_KEYS+D_KEYS+E_KEYS+V_KEYS

# A term is coefficient * (dt^nt dr^nr q_i) * (dt^mt dr^mr q_j).
Term = Tuple[complex, int, Tuple[int,int], int, Tuple[int,int]]


def canonicalize_mh_vector(mh: Mapping[str,float]) -> Dict[str,float]:
    """Map the Maxwell-Horndeski vector numbering into the 13-slot ZK schema.

    Kase-Tsujikawa (2023) uses v1..v9:
      v1 flux^2; v2 H0 flux; v3 H2 flux; v4 phi’ flux; v5 phi flux;
      v6 H0^2; v7 h1 A0; v8 A0^2; v9 A1^2.

    Zhang-Kase (2024) inserts a new flux-h1 coefficient at canonical v6 and
    therefore shifts the old algebraic vector slots by one:
      V7=MH v6, V8=MH v7, V9=MH v8, V10=MH v9,
      with V6=V11=V12=V13=0 in Maxwell-Horndeski.
    """
    out = {k: float(mh.get(k,0.0)) for k in A_KEYS+B_KEYS+C_KEYS+D_KEYS+E_KEYS}
    for i in range(1,14): out[f"v{i}"] = 0.0
    for i in range(1,6): out[f"v{i}"] = float(mh.get(f"v{i}",0.0))
    out["v6"] = 0.0
    out["v7"] = float(mh.get("v6",0.0))
    out["v8"] = float(mh.get("v7",0.0))
    out["v9"] = float(mh.get("v8",0.0))
    out["v10"] = float(mh.get("v9",0.0))
    return out


def blank_coefficients() -> Dict[str,float]:
    return {k: 0.0 for k in CANONICAL_KEYS}


def combine_coefficients(*pieces: Mapping[str,float]) -> Dict[str,float]:
    """Add already-baseline-separated contributions in the common schema."""
    out = blank_coefficients()
    for p in pieces:
        for k,v in p.items():
            if k in out: out[k] += float(v)
    return out


def _add(terms: List[Term], coef, f1, d1=(0,0), f2=None, d2=(0,0)):
    if f2 is None: f2=f1
    if coef != 0:
        terms.append((complex(coef), FI[f1], tuple(d1), FI[f2], tuple(d2)))




                                                        77
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                           Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


def build_terms(c: Mapping[str,float], L: float) -> List[Term]:
    """Build the frozen-coefficient common auxiliary-field Lagrangian."""
    q = {k: float(c.get(k,0.0)) for k in CANONICAL_KEYS}
    T: List[Term] = []

    # Lu, common Eq. (4.12)
    _add(T,q[’a1’],’H0’,(0,0),’dphi’,(0,2))
    _add(T,q[’a2’],’H0’,(0,0),’dphi’,(0,1))
    _add(T,q[’a3’],’H0’,(0,0),’H2’,(0,1))
    _add(T,L*q[’a4’],’H0’,(0,0),’h1’,(0,1))
    _add(T,q[’a5’]+L*q[’a6’],’H0’,(0,0),’dphi’,(0,0))
    _add(T,q[’a7’]+L*q[’a8’],’H0’,(0,0),’H2’,(0,0))
    _add(T,L*q[’a9’],’H0’,(0,0),’h1’,(0,0))
    _add(T,L*q[’b1’],’H1’,(0,0),’H1’,(0,0))
    _add(T,q[’b2’],’H1’,(0,0),’dphi’,(1,1))
    _add(T,q[’b3’],’H1’,(0,0),’dphi’,(1,0))
    _add(T,q[’b4’],’H1’,(0,0),’H2’,(1,0))
    _add(T,L*q[’b5’],’H1’,(0,0),’h1’,(1,0))
    _add(T,q[’c1’],’dphi’,(1,0),’H2’,(1,0))
    _add(T,q[’c2’],’H2’,(0,0),’dphi’,(0,1))
    _add(T,q[’c3’]+L*q[’c4’],’H2’,(0,0),’dphi’,(0,0))
    _add(T,L*q[’c5’],’H2’,(0,0),’h1’,(0,0))
    _add(T,q[’c6’],’H2’,(0,0),’H2’,(0,0))
    _add(T,L*q[’d1’],’h1’,(1,0),’h1’,(1,0))
    _add(T,L*q[’d2’],’h1’,(0,0),’dphi’,(0,1))
    _add(T,L*q[’d3’],’h1’,(0,0),’dphi’,(0,0))
    _add(T,L*q[’d4’],’h1’,(0,0),’h1’,(0,0))
    _add(T,q[’e1’],’dphi’,(1,0),’dphi’,(1,0))
    _add(T,q[’e2’],’dphi’,(0,1),’dphi’,(0,1))
    _add(T,q[’e3’]+L*q[’e4’],’dphi’,(0,0),’dphi’,(0,0))

    # LA after auxiliary V, common ZK Eq. (4.14).
    v1=q[’v1’]
    if abs(v1) < 1e-300:
        raise ZeroDivisionError(’v1=0: auxiliary-field representation is singular’)

    _add(T, 2*v1, ’V’,(0,0),’dA0’,(0,1))
    _add(T,-2*v1, ’V’,(0,0),’dA1’,(1,0))
    _add(T,-v1, ’V’,(0,0),’V’,(0,0))

    # S=v2 H0+v3 H2+v4 dphi’+v5 dphi+L v6 h1.
    S = [
        (q[’v2’],’H0’,(0,0)),
        (q[’v3’],’H2’,(0,0)),
        (q[’v4’],’dphi’,(0,1)),
        (q[’v5’],’dphi’,(0,0)),
        (L*q[’v6’],’h1’,(0,0)),
    ]
    for coef,field,der in S:
        _add(T,coef,’V’,(0,0),field,der) # + V S
    for i,(ci,fi,di) in enumerate(S):
        _add(T,-ci*ci/(4*v1),fi,di,fi,di)
        for cj,fj,dj in S[i+1:]:
            _add(T,-ci*cj/(2*v1),fi,di,fj,dj)

    _add(T,0.5*L*q[’v6’],’h1’,(0,0),’dA1’,(1,0))
    _add(T,q[’v7’],’H0’,(0,0),’H0’,(0,0))
    _add(T,L*q[’v8’],’h1’,(0,0),’dA0’,(0,0))
    _add(T,L*q[’v9’],’dA0’,(0,0),’dA0’,(0,0))
    _add(T,L*q[’v10’],’dA1’,(0,0),’dA1’,(0,0))
    _add(T,L*q[’v11’],’H1’,(0,0),’dA1’,(0,0))
    _add(T,L*q[’v12’],’H2’,(0,0),’dA0’,(0,0))
    _add(T,L*q[’v13’],’dphi’,(0,0),’dA0’,(0,0))
    return T


def symbol_matrix(c: Mapping[str,float], omega: float, kr: float, L: float) -> np.ndarray:
    """Euler-Lagrange Fourier symbol of the common frozen quadratic action.

    Convention q ~ exp(-i omega t + i kr r).   The result is Hermitian up to


                                                       78
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                          Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


    floating roundoff for real coefficients and real omega,kr.
    """
    dt = -1j*omega
    dr = 1j*kr
    P = np.zeros((len(FIELDS),len(FIELDS)),complex)
    for coef,i,a,j,b in build_terms(c,L):
        na=sum(a); nb=sum(b)
        dprod=(dt**(a[0]+b[0]))*(dr**(a[1]+b[1]))
        if i==j:
             P[i,i] += coef*((-1)**na + (-1)**nb)*dprod
        else:
             P[i,j] += coef*((-1)**na)*dprod
             P[j,i] += coef*((-1)**nb)*dprod
    return P


def h0_constraint_expected(c: Mapping[str,float], omega:float, kr:float, L:float) -> np.ndarray:
    """Expected frozen H0 row from direct variation of the auxiliary action."""
    q={k:float(c.get(k,0.0)) for k in CANONICAL_KEYS}
    dt=-1j*omega; dr=1j*kr
    row=np.zeros(len(FIELDS),complex)
    v1=q[’v1’]
    row[FI[’dphi’]] = q[’a1’]*dr**2 + (q[’a2’]-q[’v2’]*q[’v4’]/(2*v1))*dr + q[’a5’]+L*q[’a6’]-q[’v2’]*q[’v5’
     ]/(2*v1)
    row[FI[’H2’]] = q[’a3’]*dr + q[’a7’]+L*q[’a8’]-q[’v2’]*q[’v3’]/(2*v1)
    row[FI[’h1’]] = L*q[’a4’]*dr + L*(q[’a9’]-q[’v2’]*q[’v6’]/(2*v1))
    row[FI[’V’]] = q[’v2’]
    # H0^2 cancels iff v7=v2^2/(4v1)
    row[FI[’H0’]] = 2*(q[’v7’]-q[’v2’]**2/(4*v1))
    return row


def validate_common_structure(c: Mapping[str,float], r:float|None=None, f:float|None=None,
                              tol:float=1e-10) -> Dict[str,float|bool]:
    q={k:float(c.get(k,0.0)) for k in CANONICAL_KEYS}
    out={}
    out[’H0_square_identity_residual’]=q[’v7’]-q[’v2’]**2/(4*q[’v1’]) if q[’v1’] else np.nan
    if r is not None:
        out[’a3_plus_r_a4’]=q[’a3’]+r*q[’a4’]
    if r is not None and f is not None:
        out[’b4_minus_2r_a4_over_f’]=q[’b4’]-2*r*q[’a4’]/f
        out[’b5_plus_a4_over_f’]=q[’b5’]+q[’a4’]/f
        out[’d1_minus_a4_over_2f’]=q[’d1’]-q[’a4’]/(2*f)
    finite=[abs(float(v)) for v in out.values() if np.isfinite(v)]
    out[’structural_identities_pass’]=bool(not finite or max(finite)<tol)
    return out


def self_test() -> Dict[str,float|bool]:
    rng=np.random.default_rng(20260912)
    c={k:float(rng.normal()) for k in CANONICAL_KEYS}
    c[’v1’]=1.7
    c[’v2’]=0.8
    c[’v7’]=c[’v2’]**2/(4*c[’v1’])
    r=1.51; f=.42
    c[’a4’]=.31; c[’a3’]=-r*c[’a4’]
    c[’b4’]=2*r*c[’a4’]/f; c[’b5’]=-c[’a4’]/f; c[’d1’]=c[’a4’]/(2*f)
    om=.73; kr=1.17; L=30.0
    P=symbol_matrix(c,om,kr,L)
    expected=h0_constraint_expected(c,om,kr,L)
    herm=np.max(np.abs(P-P.conj().T))
    h0err=np.max(np.abs(P[FI[’H0’]]-expected))
    ids=validate_common_structure(c,r,f)
    return {
        ’hermiticity_error’:float(herm),
        ’H0_constraint_row_error’:float(h0err),
        ’H0_diagonal_abs’:float(abs(P[FI[’H0’],FI[’H0’]])),
        ’structural_identities_pass’:bool(ids[’structural_identities_pass’]),
        ’pass’:bool(herm<1e-12 and h0err<1e-12 and abs(P[FI[’H0’],FI[’H0’]])<1e-12 and ids[’
     structural_identities_pass’])


                                                      79
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                            Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


       }


if __name__==’__main__’:
    print(self_test())




C.11          First-stage jet-aware constraint reducer
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Jet-aware first-stage constraint reducer for the common Horndeski + U(1)-SVT
even-parity quadratic action.

Why this module exists
----------------------
For the constrained even system, freezing radial coefficient functions before
eliminating H0,H1,H2,h1,dA0,dA1 loses principal information. The published
reduced kinetic matrix contains radial jets such as a4’ and K2’.

This module therefore works on radial coefficient PROFILES and performs the
generalized-psi H0 reduction while retaining coefficient derivatives.

Common generalized dynamical variable
--------------------------------------
    psi = H2 + q h1 + p dphi’
    p = a1/a3
    q = L a4/a3

For EH + genuine SVT:
    a1 = 0, a3 = -r a4
so
    psi = H2 - (L/r) h1.

After substituting
    H2 = psi - q h1 - p dphi’
into the H0 constraint, the dphi’’ and h1’ terms cancel identically.

The remaining H0 constraint is algebraic in h1:
    0 = a3 psi’
        + B_eff dphi’
        + C_phi dphi
        + C_H2 psi
        + D_h1 h1
        + v2 V

with
       B_eff = B - a3 p’ - C_H2 p
       B      = a2 - v2 v4/(2 v1)
       C_phi = a5 + L a6 - v2 v5/(2 v1)
       C_H2   = a7 + L a8 - v2 v3/(2 v1)
       C_h1   = L (a9 - v2 v6/(2 v1))
       D_h1   = C_h1 - a3 q’ - C_H2 q.

Thus, where D_h1 != 0,
    h1 = -(a3 psi’ + B_eff dphi’ + C_phi dphi + C_H2 psi + v2 V)/D_h1.

This is the first elimination step for a common same-action HSVT reducer.
"""

from __future__ import annotations
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Dict
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from scipy.interpolate import CubicSpline

REQUIRED = (


                                                      80
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                          Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


    "a1","a2","a3","a4","a5","a6","a7","a8","a9",
    "v1","v2","v3","v4","v5","v6"
)

def radial_derivative(r: np.ndarray, y: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    r = np.asarray(r, float)
    y = np.asarray(y, float)
    order = np.argsort(r)
    rr = r[order]
    yy = y[order]
    if np.any(np.diff(rr) <= 0):
        raise ValueError("r values must be unique")
    dy = CubicSpline(rr, yy)(rr, 1)
    out = np.empty_like(dy)
    out[order] = dy
    return out

def generalized_psi_h0_reduction(df: pd.DataFrame, L: float,
                                 r_col: str = "x") -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Return the jet-aware H0 algebraic-constraint coefficients."""
    missing = [k for k in REQUIRED if k not in df.columns]
    if missing:
        raise KeyError(f"Missing coefficients: {missing}")

    r = df[r_col].to_numpy(float)
    qv = {k: df[k].to_numpy(float) for k in REQUIRED}

    if np.any(np.abs(qv["a3"]) < 1e-14):
        raise ZeroDivisionError("a3 crosses zero")
    if np.any(np.abs(qv["v1"]) < 1e-14):
        raise ZeroDivisionError("v1 crosses zero")

    p = qv["a1"]/qv["a3"]
    q = L*qv["a4"]/qv["a3"]
    pp = radial_derivative(r, p)
    qp = radial_derivative(r, q)

    B = qv["a2"] - qv["v2"]*qv["v4"]/(2*qv["v1"])
    Cphi = qv["a5"] + L*qv["a6"] - qv["v2"]*qv["v5"]/(2*qv["v1"])
    CH2 = qv["a7"] + L*qv["a8"] - qv["v2"]*qv["v3"]/(2*qv["v1"])
    Ch1 = L*(qv["a9"] - qv["v2"]*qv["v6"]/(2*qv["v1"]))

    Beff = B - qv["a3"]*pp - CH2*p
    Dh1 = Ch1 - qv["a3"]*qp - CH2*q

    # Explicit cancellation residuals before dropping the higher derivatives.
    dphi2_res = qv["a1"] - qv["a3"]*p
    h1prime_res = L*qv["a4"] - qv["a3"]*q

    result = pd.DataFrame({
        r_col: r,
        "p_a1_over_a3": p,
        "p_prime": pp,
        "q_La4_over_a3": q,
        "q_prime": qp,
        "B_raw": B,
        "B_eff": Beff,
        "C_phi": Cphi,
        "C_H2": CH2,
        "C_h1": Ch1,
        "D_h1_pivot": Dh1,
        "dphi_second_derivative_cancellation": dphi2_res,
        "h1_prime_cancellation": h1prime_res,
    })
    if "u" in df.columns:
        result.insert(0, "u", df["u"].to_numpy(float))
    return result

def h1_solution_coefficients(reduced: pd.DataFrame,
                             source_a3: np.ndarray,


                                                        81
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                          Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


                            source_v2: np.ndarray) -> pd.DataFrame:
   """Coefficients of h1 in terms of (psi’, dphi’, dphi, psi, V)."""
   D = reduced["D_h1_pivot"].to_numpy(float)
   return pd.DataFrame({
       "coeff_psi_prime": -np.asarray(source_a3,float)/D,
       "coeff_dphi_prime": -reduced["B_eff"].to_numpy(float)/D,
       "coeff_dphi": -reduced["C_phi"].to_numpy(float)/D,
       "coeff_psi": -reduced["C_H2"].to_numpy(float)/D,
       "coeff_V": -np.asarray(source_v2,float)/D,
   })

Abstract
We investigate whether a prescribed regular, static, spherically symmetric strong-field geometry
arising within the Segmented Spacetime (SSZ) program can be realized as a dynamically admissible
solution of a covariant scalar–vector–tensor theory rather than merely as an effective metric ansatz.
The geometry studied here is the frozen P5 background, described by temporal and radial metric
functions f(r) and h(r), together with a monotonic scalar profile \phi(r)\equiv\Xi(r). Its
strong-field region contains two circular null orbits: an outer unstable light ring at

                                                    𝑟
                                               𝑥≡      = 1.5,
                                                    𝑟𝑠

and an inner stable light ring at


                                           𝑥s ≃ 1.41616064.

The outer orbit reproduces the general-relativistic Schwarzschild shadow scale and the corresponding
eikonal Lyapunov scale, while the inner orbit forms a shallow stable null potential well with a relative
depth of approximately 4.18\times10^{-3}.
The frozen geometry is not treated here as an arbitrary numerical triplet. Its current source-locked
closure can be written in terms of a primary susceptibility \chi=d\Xi/du=(1-\Xi)^{3/2}\mathcal
P_5(\Xi), with f=(1+\Xi)^{-2} and a radial closure generated by the ratio \chi/\chi_{\rm GR}.
Independent regression reproduces the frozen temporal and radial metric profiles at machine preci-
sion. The same closure is horizonless (f>0, h>0), regular at the center, and reproduces Schwarzschild
through cubic order in the weak-field compactness expansion. At the outer light ring it additionally
matches f=h=1/3 and the Schwarzschild curvature of the dimensionless null potential, while the
inner light ring remains a genuinely new stable-trapping structure.
The purpose of the present work is not merely to diagnose the geodesic properties of this metric, but
to construct explicit local action jets capable of supporting the same background while satisfying
the no-ghost and Laplacian-stability conditions of scalar–tensor and genuine U(1) gauge-invariant
scalar–vector–tensor theories. The construction is divided into three dynamically distinct but over-
lapping radial domains. The weak-to-intermediate exterior is carried by a finite Horndeski G_3-G_4
sector. The central strong-field light-ring region is realized by a genuine scalar–vector–tensor inter-
action with background-integrable action jets. The regular inner region is supported by a quartic
Horndeski core. These are not left as unrelated effective descriptions: using a scalar-coordinate
C^\infty partition, background re-solving, transverse action-jet controls and the common unre-
duced even-parity constraint system, we construct a single smooth local same-action continuation
through both overlap bands.

                                                     7
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                 Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16

demonstrating shallow stable trapping.

For the genuine-SVT strong-field lobe we explicitly solve the scalar background equation for the
required f_{3,X} profile instead of treating it as an arbitrary perturbative control. On the interval

                                                     𝑟𝑠
                                       0.61 ≤ 𝑢 ≡       ≤ 0.715,
                                                     𝑟

corresponding to


                                    1.3986014 ≲ 𝑥 ≲ 1.6393443,

the resulting action section is background integrable with scalar-equation residual below approxi-
mately 2\times10^{-12}. This interval contains both light rings and overlaps the regular Horndeski
core.
Using the odd- and even-parity perturbation formalism for general U(1)-gauge-invariant scalar–
vector–tensor theories, we find positive no-ghost and radial-gradient margins throughout this lobe.
After correcting the exact Zhang–Kase definitions of P_1 and the even-sector kinetic combination
\mathcal K, the internal identity


                                             𝒦 = 𝑒1 √𝑓ℎ 𝜙′2

is reproduced numerically with maximum residual approximately


                                               2.2 × 10−10 .

The corrected kinetic quantity satisfies


                                           10.54 ≲ 𝒦 ≲ 137.88.

The three radial squared propagation speeds satisfy

SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                      Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16

                     𝑐𝑟,𝑇 > 1.005,     0.9037 ≲ 𝑐𝑟,𝑆 ≲ 1.0614,       𝑐𝑟,𝑉 > 9.45,

over the complete lobe. The coupled even-parity angular sector is also positive, with minimum lower
angular eigenvalue

                                             2
                                            𝑐Ω,− ≃ 84.91,

and minimum upper eigenvalue

                                             2
                                            𝑐Ω,+ ≃ 643.14.

The odd-parity tensor and vector sectors likewise remain free of local ghost and Laplacian instabil-
ities. These results establish a constructive local stability witness for a genuine-SVT realization of
the complete P5 light-ring region.
A separate quartic-Horndeski witness covers the center through


                                            𝑥 ≃ 1.40845,

thereby creating a true inner overlap with the genuine-SVT lobe. The exterior Horndeski scalar
carrier can furthermore be continued through both light rings while maintaining a positive scalar
no-ghost quantity. The two overlap bands therefore supply the domains on which the local repre-
sentatives are promoted to one smooth same-action Horndeski–SVT section.
We show that the corresponding perturbation problem must be assembled at the unreduced action
level. Separately reduced Maxwell–Horndeski and genuine-SVT kinetic matrices cannot consistently
be added after constraint elimination. A common generalized variable,

                                                𝐿𝑎4     𝑎
                                     𝜓 = 𝐻2 +       ℎ1 + 1 𝛿𝜙′ ,
                                                 𝑎3     𝑎3

provides the appropriate bridge. In the pure Einstein-plus-SVT limit it reduces exactly to

                                                       𝐿
                                           𝜓 = 𝐻2 −      ℎ .
                                                       𝑟 1

The common jet-aware even-parity reduction is constructed explicitly. The H_0 constraint yields a
regular solution for h_1; the remaining variables H_1, \delta A_0, and \delta A_1 are eliminated
by exact Schur-complement formulas. The corresponding pivots are all finite and nonzero on the
certified reference construction. In the pure genuine-SVT limit the coupled H_1–\delta A_1 deter-
minant reduces exactly to \Delta_V=-4C_{\rm odd}, tying even-constraint invertibility directly to
the already positive odd-sector stability margin.
The resulting construction closes the local same-action existence problem. A smooth covariant
Horndeski–SVT action can be defined in a tubular neighborhood of the frozen P5 background curve
such that the background equations are satisfied, the pure local representatives are recovered with all
finite endpoint jets, the nondynamical even-parity constraints remain invertible, and the odd/even
principal-symbol targets remain inside their strict healthy domain through both handovers. This



                                                  93
                                                  10
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                      Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


is an existence and local-stability closure, not a claim of a unique fundamental action. Finite-
\ell spectra, stable-trapping timescales, nonlinear evolution, causal-cone interpretation and UV
completion remain distinct post-closure questions.




1. Introduction
1.1 Geometry is not yet dynamics
A spacetime metric may possess attractive geometrical properties without corresponding to a dy-
namically viable theory.
This distinction becomes particularly important whenever a proposed strong-field geometry departs
from the standard Schwarzschild or Kerr solutions of general relativity. It is comparatively straight-
forward to prescribe metric functions that are asymptotically acceptable, regular near the center,
reproduce familiar weak-field behavior, or generate interesting null-geodesic structures. It is sub-
stantially harder to demonstrate that the same geometry can arise from a covariant field theory
whose physical perturbations carry positive kinetic energy, whose characteristic speeds are real,
and whose constraint structure does not become singular precisely in the region where the geometry
becomes phenomenologically interesting.
The present work addresses this second question for the P5 geometry developed within the Seg-
mented Spacetime framework.
Our starting point is a static, spherically symmetric metric of the form

                                                       𝑑𝑟2
                                  𝑑𝑠2 = −𝑓(𝑟) 𝑑𝑡2 +         + 𝑟2 𝑑Ω2 ,
                                                       ℎ(𝑟)

where


                                      𝑑Ω2 = 𝑑𝜃2 + sin2 𝜃 𝑑𝜑2 .

We use the dimensionless variables

                                           𝑟             𝑟𝑠  1
                                      𝑥=      ,     𝑢=      = ,
                                           𝑟𝑠            𝑟   𝑥

where r_s provides the natural radial scale of the P5 solution.
The scalar field used in the covariant reconstruction is identified with the monotonically varying
SSZ background variable,


                                             𝜙(𝑟) ≡ Ξ(𝑟).

The functions


                                           {𝑓(𝑟), ℎ(𝑟), Ξ(𝑟)}




                                                  94
                                                  11
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                   Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


are regarded throughout this analysis as the frozen geometrical target. We do not tune the
metric in response to stability failures. Instead, the problem is posed in the opposite direction:
      Given the P5 geometry, can one construct a covariant action whose background equations
      reproduce that geometry and whose perturbations are locally healthy?
That distinction is central to the logic of this paper.
The metric is therefore not inferred from a convenient scalar–tensor theory. The theory is recon-
structed around the metric.
This makes the calculation closer in spirit to an inverse problem than to the conventional procedure
of selecting a Lagrangian and subsequently solving its background equations.



1.2 Why the light-ring region is the critical test
The P5 geometry possesses a particularly useful strong-field structure because it contains two cir-
cular null orbits.
The outer orbit occurs at


                                              𝑥out = 1.5

or equivalently

                                                     2
                                               𝑢out = .
                                                     3

It is unstable.
The second orbit occurs at


                                          𝑥in ≃ 1.41616064 ,

corresponding to


                                           𝑢in ≃ 0.706135.

It is stable.
The coexistence of an outer unstable and inner stable light ring provides a much stronger diagnostic
of the strong-field theory than weak-field agreement alone.
Circular null trajectories are associated with extrema of the effective null potential. For a metric
written in areal radius and in the form above, the usual circular-null condition is determined by the
temporal potential,

                                           𝑑 𝑓(𝑟)
                                              ( 2 ) = 0,
                                           𝑑𝑟   𝑟

which is equivalent, whenever f\neq0, to


                                                  95
                                                  12
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                    Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16



                                         𝑟𝑓 ′ (𝑟) − 2𝑓(𝑟) = 0.

The P5 target possesses two solutions to this condition.
The outer solution reproduces the desired Schwarzschild-like optical scale. In particular, the outer
light ring is located at the same dimensionless areal radius as the Schwarzschild photon sphere when
r_s=2GM,

                                              3
                                         𝑟ph = 𝑟𝑠 = 3𝐺𝑀 .
                                              2

The corresponding shadow-impact scale and eikonal Lyapunov scale have been matched by construc-
tion in the frozen P5 geometry.
The inner solution is qualitatively different. Since it is stable, it corresponds to a local minimum
rather than maximum of the relevant null effective potential. The well is shallow: the relative depth
obtained from the frozen P5 profile is approximately


                                        Δnull ≃ 4.177 × 10−3 ,

or about 0.42\%.
The presence of a stable light ring is dynamically nontrivial.
Stable trapping can lead to long-lived perturbative structures even when no local ghost or Laplacian
instability exists. Consequently, the present perturbative analysis does not claim that positivity of
the local principal symbol settles the complete dynamical fate of the stable light ring.
It instead addresses a logically prior question:


             Does the local covariant field theory itself become sick at the light rings?

The answer obtained here is no.
Neither light ring forces the kinetic determinant of the full local scalar–vector–tensor construction
to vanish, and the explicit strong-field genuine-SVT realization satisfies the available local odd- and
even-parity no-ghost and gradient conditions across both orbits.
Long-time trapping, finite-\ell resonances, quasinormal spectra, and nonlinear evolution remain
separate problems.



1.3 Why Horndeski alone is not enough for the entire construction
Scalar–tensor theories of Horndeski type provide an exceptionally broad class of second-order co-
variant theories. In their standard form they can be written schematically as

                                                     5
                                                 √
                                      𝑆𝐻 = ∫ 𝑑4 𝑥 −𝑔 ∑ ℒ𝑖 ,
                                                         𝑖=2


where the four functions


                                                   96
                                                   13
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                          Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16



                        𝐺2 (𝜙, 𝑋),      𝐺3 (𝜙, 𝑋),        𝐺4 (𝜙, 𝑋),      𝐺5 (𝜙, 𝑋)

govern the scalar and curvature interactions, with

                                               1
                                          𝑋 = − ∇𝜇 𝜙∇𝜇 𝜙.
                                               2

For static spherical backgrounds,


                                               𝜙 = 𝜙(𝑟),

and therefore

                                               1
                                          𝑋 = − ℎ(𝑟)𝜙′ (𝑟)2 .
                                               2

Horndeski theory is suﬀiciently flexible to support large classes of nontrivial compact-object solutions
while retaining second-order equations of motion.
However, our reconstruction experiments show that a restricted scalar-only realization is not natu-
rally adequate throughout the P5 strong-field domain.
In particular, simple sectors such as


                                        𝐺2 (𝜙, 𝑋, 𝐹 ) + 𝐺4 (𝜙)𝑅

without the required derivative scalar interactions fail to provide a uniformly healthy realization
over the relevant radial range.
The exterior admits a useful finite G_3-supported solution.
The center admits a quartic-Horndeski witness.
The intermediate two-light-ring domain, however, becomes considerably more tractable once genuine
scalar–vector–tensor interactions are allowed.
This motivates the hybrid construction developed below.



1.4 Genuine U(1) scalar–vector–tensor interactions
We introduce a gauge field


                                                     𝐴𝜇

with field strength


                                        𝐹𝜇𝜈 = ∇𝜇 𝐴𝜈 − ∇𝜈 𝐴𝜇 ,

and impose the usual U(1) gauge symmetry


                                                     97
                                                     14
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                       Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16



                                          𝐴𝜇 → 𝐴𝜇 + ∇𝜇 Λ.

On the static spherical background we use the electric ansatz


                                         𝐴𝜇 = (𝐴0 (𝑟), 0, 0, 0) .

In addition to

                                               1
                                          𝑋 = − ∇𝜇 𝜙∇𝜇 𝜙,
                                               2

one may construct the gauge invariants

                                                1
                                           𝐹 = − 𝐹𝜇𝜈 𝐹 𝜇𝜈 ,
                                                4

and


                                        𝑌 = ∇𝜇 𝜙∇𝜈 𝜙𝐹 𝜇𝛼 𝐹 𝜈 𝛼 ,

up to convention-dependent normalizations.
For the purely electric static background used here, the relevant background combinations reduce
to functions of A_0'(r), f(r), h(r), and \phi'(r).
The most general U(1)-gauge-invariant scalar–vector–tensor theories with second-order equations
of motion propagate five physical degrees of freedom and possess nontrivial odd- and even-parity
perturbation sectors. Zhang and Kase derived the corresponding ghost and Laplacian-stability
conditions for static spherically symmetric backgrounds, including the coupled even sector.
For \ell\ge2, Maxwell–Horndeski theory likewise contains two propagating odd-parity modes and
three propagating even-parity modes, and Kase and Tsujikawa derived the associated radial and
angular stability conditions.
More recent work has also demonstrated that gauge-invariant SVT extensions can retain a gen-
uine Horndeski scalar–tensor sector while introducing additional vector interactions, reinforcing
the structural plausibility of treating Horndeski and genuine SVT interactions within one common
action rather than as unrelated effective descriptions.
This motivates the action architecture

                                                    genuine
                                          𝑆 = 𝑆H + 𝑆SVT     ,

with shared Einstein/Maxwell/scalar pieces counted only once.
This point will become crucial later.



1.5 The central methodological diﬀiculty
The main technical problem is not simply finding enough free functions.

                                                   98
                                                   15
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                      Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


There are many free functions.
The real diﬀiculty is satisfying several logically distinct requirements simultaneously.
The target metric must solve the background equations.
The scalar profile must solve its own equation rather than merely the metric equations.
The vector background must remain regular.
The action jets used to repair perturbative instabilities must correspond to derivatives of a consistent
local action.
The kinetic matrices must remain nonsingular.
The physical eigenmodes must have positive kinetic energy.
Their squared radial and angular propagation speeds must remain positive.
The construction must remain finite across both light rings.
And finally, independently constructed Horndeski and genuine-SVT regions must be capable of
belonging to one common action.
An arbitrary pointwise assignment of perturbative coeﬀicients would not solve this problem.
The strategy developed here is therefore based on background-integrable action jets and
background-null perturbative controls.
The former ensure that the action derivatives required by the background equations arise from a
consistent local functional structure.
The latter use directions in higher action-derivative space that do not change the already-fixed
background solution but do change the quadratic perturbation operator.
This separation is what makes the construction tractable.




2. Scope of the Present Result
Before presenting the calculation, it is useful to state precisely what has and has not been established.
The strongest result obtained in this work is local and perturbative.
We construct a genuine-SVT action section covering the entire two-light-ring region and demon-
strate:


                                       background integrability,


                                 positive odd-parity kinetic structure,


                        positive odd radial and angular gradient conditions,


                                   positive even no-ghost structure,


                                                   99
                                                   16
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                      Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16



                            positive tensor, scalar, and vector radial 𝑐𝑟2 ,

and


                             positive coupled even angular eigenvalues.

The solution therefore passes the local ghost/Laplacian/hyperbolicity tests available in the employed
perturbative formalism.
We do not claim from this alone that the system possesses a globally well-posed nonlinear evolution
for arbitrary data.
We do not claim that all characteristic speeds are subluminal.
Indeed, several reconstructed propagation speeds are substantially larger than unity.
This does not constitute a Laplacian instability: the corresponding c^2 remains positive. It does,
however, create a distinct question concerning causal interpretation, effective-field-theory validity,
and the relation of the characteristic cones to the matter light cone.
That question must be treated separately.
Likewise, the present calculation does not yet contain a complete finite-\ell quasinormal-mode
spectrum.
It does not yet determine whether stable trapping generates parametrically long-lived modes.
It does not constitute a nonlinear stability theorem.
The local same-action handover problem is addressed explicitly below. The result is stronger than
the existence of separate healthy patches:
The frozen P5 geometry admits a smooth local same-action Horndeski–SVT re-
alization across the exterior, both light rings, and the regular core. The com-
plete two-light-ring interval remains inside the known local odd- and even-parity
ghost/Laplacian stability domain, and every nondynamical even-parity pivot ex-
posed by the common unreduced reduction is finite and nonzero on the certified
reference construction.
What remains after this closure is not another local patching step but the global dynamical program:
finite-\ell mode spectra, stable-trapping lifetimes, nonlinear evolution, causal-cone analysis and UV
completion.

3.8 Reproducibility status
The P5 susceptibility, temporal closure, and radial closure have been independently regressed against
the frozen source profile. The resulting geometry can now be regenerated from the equations in this
section and the boundary condition \Xi(0)=0.
The historical SSZ 1.8r_s–2.2r_s Hermite bridge remains important as provenance and as a varia-
tional template for smooth handovers, but it is not inserted into the present strong-field P5 geometry
as a fundamental field equation. The distinction between the historical bridge and the current P5
closure is maintained throughout the rest of the paper.

4.2 Outer unstable light ring
The outer solution is


                                              𝑥out = 1.5 .

Its instability is determined by the sign of the second variation of the null effective potential.
This light ring was used as one of the principal geometrical calibration points of the P5 strong-field
profile.
At this orbit the construction reproduces the desired Schwarzschild optical scale.
In the units r_s=2GM,


                                         𝑟out = 1.5𝑟𝑠 = 3𝐺𝑀 ,

coinciding with the Schwarzschild photon-sphere radius.
The corresponding critical impact parameter is therefore arranged to reproduce the Schwarzschild
shadow scale.
The eikonal instability rate, characterized by the radial Lyapunov exponent of the orbit, is also
matched at this outer ring.



                                                  108
                                                   25
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                       Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


The significance of the present dynamical reconstruction is that this geometrical agreement does
not coincide with a degeneracy of the local field-theory kinetic matrix.
A full-SVT background pivot investigated during the construction has determinant

                                                     √
                                        det ℳ = 𝑓 5/2 ℎ 𝜙′2 𝑟8 ,

up to the normalization adopted for the particular local reconstruction variables.
Crucially, this expression contains no factor


                                                𝑟𝑓 ′ − 2𝑓.

Hence the light-ring equation is not automatically a perturbative determinant zero.
Numerically the pivot remains finite at the outer orbit.
This already indicates that the optical critical point of null geodesics and the constraint structure
of the covariant field theory are logically distinct.



4.3 Inner stable light ring
The second root occurs at


                                            𝑥in ≃ 1.41616064

or


                                             𝑢in ≃ 0.706135.

The second derivative of the null potential has the opposite sign from that of the outer orbit, making
this solution stable against infinitesimal radial null displacements at the geodesic level.
The associated null well is shallow.
The numerically reconstructed relative depth is approximately


                                         Δwell ≃ 4.177 × 10−3 .

This shallow trapping region is one of the most interesting aspects of the geometry.
It also forces us to distinguish three different notions of stability.
First, there is geodesic stability of a circular null trajectory.
Second, there is local field-theory stability, meaning absence of ghosts and imaginary character-
istic speeds.
Third, there is global dynamical stability, which concerns the long-time evolution of finite per-
turbations and includes possible effects of trapping and mode accumulation.
The inner light ring is stable in the first sense.

                                                     109
                                                      26
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                      Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


The genuine-SVT realization constructed below is healthy in the second sense.
The third question remains open.




5. Covariant Theory Architecture
5.1 Full Horndeski sector

                                        ℒ3 = −𝐺3 (𝜙, 𝑋)□𝜙,


                           ℒ4 = 𝐺4 (𝜙, 𝑋)𝑅 + 𝐺4,𝑋 [(□𝜙)2 − 𝜙;𝜇𝜈 𝜙;𝜇𝜈 ] ,

and

5.3 One common action and the no-double-counting convention
The most useful architecture for the final P5 completion is

                                              5
                                         √             genuine
                               𝑆 = ∫ 𝑑4 𝑥 −𝑔 [∑ ℒH
                                                 𝑖 + ΔℒSVT ] .
                                                    𝑖=2


The symbol \Delta is important. The general SVT f_2 sector can itself contain pure scalar and
Maxwell pieces already represented in the Horndeski/Maxwell baseline. In the hybrid bookkeeping
these shared pieces are counted exactly once.
Equivalently, at the level of any unreduced quadratic-action coeﬀicient,


                                 𝐶HSVT = 𝐶MH + (𝐶SVT − 𝐶shared ) .

This combination must be performed before constraint elimination.
The P5 construction therefore distinguishes three kinds of action data:
   1. background-constrained jets, fixed by the metric, scalar, vector and background equations;
   2. background-null transverse jets, unconstrained by the one-dimensional background curve
      but visible to perturbations;
   3. handover jets, generated when different healthy local representatives are embedded into one
      scalar-coordinate partition and differentiated.
This classification is the basis of the same-action closure proved in Sections 16–21.



5.4 Static field equations and integrability hierarchy
On the static spherical ansatz the independent background equations may be organized as


                           ℰ00 = 0,      ℰ11 = 0,         ℰ𝜙 = 0,    ℰ𝐴 = 0.

The reconstruction does not use only the metric equations. In the strong-field lobe the metric
equations determine the required on-shell values of f_2 and f_{2,X}, the vector equation fixes
the electric branch/current condition, and the scalar equation is retained as a nontrivial first-order
integrability equation for f_{3,X}.
This ordering prevents a common inverse-construction error: a set of perturbatively convenient
pointwise jets is not accepted unless it can be lifted to a local action section that also satisfies the
background scalar and vector equations.
The resulting theory is therefore reconstructed around the frozen P5 geometry, but it is not merely
fitted to a perturbation matrix.

6. Exterior Horndeski Reconstruction
6.1 Motivation
The genuine-SVT vector background becomes weak near its natural onset.
Trying to force the complete strong-field stabilization problem onto pure Einstein-plus-SVT precisely
where


                                                  𝐴′0 → 0

can drive required interaction derivatives to unnaturally large values.
The correct solution is not to insist that one sector carry every radius.
Instead, the exterior scalar–tensor sector carries the solution through the vector onset.
The genuine-SVT interactions then become increasingly important inward.



6.2 Correct general-f\neq h no-ghost relation
For the restricted luminal exterior carrier,


                                      𝐺4 = 𝐺4 (𝜙),          𝐺5 = 0,

the scalar kinetic combination can be written in terms of an auxiliary function Q(r).
A previously used simplified expression was valid only in the special case


                                                  𝑓 = ℎ.

The frozen P5 background does not satisfy this equality throughout the relevant exterior region.
Using the general Maxwell–Horndeski relation, define

                                                        √𝑓/ℎ
                                               𝑦(𝑟) =        .
                                                         𝑄

The scalar no-ghost reconstruction becomes a first-order equation


                                            1 𝑓
                                        𝑦′ = √ (𝐾 + 𝐻) ,
                                            2 ℎ

where


                                                 𝐻 = 2𝐺4

for the restricted carrier.

SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                             Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


Imposing the regular endpoint conditions fixes a positive constant scalar kinetic margin of approxi-
mately


                                        𝐾ext ≃ 9.7423 × 10−3 .

This correction is important conceptually.
The exterior Horndeski witness survives the use of the full f\neq h geometry.



6.3 Continuation through the strong-field region
The corrected Horndeski carrier can be continued inward while remaining finite and retaining posi-
tive scalar kinetic energy.
In particular, a smooth H(r) profile can be constructed between the outer onset and the quartic-core
boundary such that the associated cubic interaction G_{3,X} remains finite.
This continuation passes through


                                                  𝑥 = 1.5

and


                                            𝑥 ≃ 1.41616064.

Thus no singular Horndeski control pivot occurs at either light ring.
A direct full-Horndeski principal-control rank test was carried out using a nonredundant set of
targets,


                                            (𝐹 , 𝐻, 𝑎1 , 𝑐4 , 𝑐2 ),

and controls drawn from


               𝐺4,𝑋 ,   𝐺4,𝑋𝑋 ,   𝐺4,𝜙𝑋 ,    𝐺5,𝜙 ,    𝐺5,𝜙𝑋 ,        𝐺5,𝑋 ,   𝐺5,𝑋𝑋 ,   𝐺2,𝑋𝑋 .

The control map retains full rank across the onset-to-core interval.
The apparent degeneracy that appears if one attempts to control redundant tensor combinations
precisely at


                                              𝑟𝑓 ′ − 2𝑓 = 0

is therefore a target-space redundancy associated with the photon-sphere condition, not a genuine
disappearance of the Horndeski action controls.
This will become important when discussing the hybrid light-ring transition.

                                            𝑞(𝑟) ≡ 𝑓3,𝑋 ∣bg .

After substituting all other chosen jets and implementing the background-chain-rule relations, the

This is one of the most important constructive steps in the calculation.
A pointwise assignment of f_{3,X} could always be used to alter a perturbation coeﬀicient.
It would not demonstrate that the assigned quantity belongs to an action whose scalar equation is
solved by the P5 profile.
Solving the ODE does.
