8. Tubular Reconstruction and Background-Null Controls
8.1 Why higher action derivatives are not fixed by the background
The static background traces only a one-dimensional trajectory through the multidimensional in-
variant space


                                                 (𝜙, 𝑋, 𝐹 , 𝑌 ).

Knowledge of the value and first directional derivative of an action function along this curve does
not determine all derivatives transverse to the curve.
For example, suppose


                                          𝑓2 = 𝑓2 (𝜙, 𝑋, 𝐹 , 𝑌 ).

The background equations constrain combinations involving quantities such as


                                        𝑓2,𝑋 ,       𝑓2,𝐹 ,        𝑓2,𝑌 ,

evaluated on the trajectory.
They do not uniquely determine the entire Hessian

                                            𝑓2,𝑋𝑋 𝑓2,𝑋𝐹             𝑓2,𝑋𝑌
                                           ⎛                               ⎞
                                   ∇2 𝑓2 = ⎜
                                           ⎜ 𝑓2,𝑋𝐹 𝑓2,𝐹 𝐹           𝑓2,𝐹 𝑌 ⎟
                                                                           ⎟.
                                           ⎝𝑓2,𝑋𝑌 𝑓2,𝐹 𝑌            𝑓2,𝑌 𝑌 ⎠

Those transverse second derivatives enter the perturbation operator.
This creates a natural set of controls that can change stability while leaving the background un-
changed.

10.1 Why this is the decisive sector
The even-parity system is substantially more complicated than the odd sector because the scalar
perturbation couples to the even gravitational and vector modes.
For \ell\ge2, the complete theory contains three physical even-parity propagating degrees of free-
dom.
Their no-ghost and gradient conditions cannot generally be inferred from the separate scalar, vector,
and tensor subsectors.
The coupled kinetic and angular matrices must be analyzed.
This makes the successful even-sector reconstruction the strongest local result of the present work.

14. Regular Quartic-Horndeski Core
14.1 Why a separate core carrier is used
The cubic G_3 scalar carrier that works eﬀiciently in the exterior cannot be extended arbitrarily
deeply into the center while retaining the desired integrated stability budget.
Dedicated endpoint-integral and handover scans show that the available scalar G_3 stability budget
becomes negative for suﬀiciently deep interior starting points.
This does not indicate a failure of Horndeski theory.
It indicates that the wrong Horndeski operator is being asked to carry the center.
The regular core is instead represented by a quartic-Horndeski construction.



14.2 Core domain
The present core witness covers approximately


                                       10−6 ≲ 𝑥 ≤ 1.4084507042.

Its outer boundary corresponds to


                                                 𝑢 = 0.71.

                                                      130
                                                       47
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                       Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


The central SVT lobe extends to


                                              𝑢 = 0.715,

or


                                           𝑥 ≃ 1.3986014.

Consequently, the two constructions overlap on


                                       1.39860 ≲ 𝑥 ≲ 1.40845.

This is a genuine overlap, not merely contact at one radius.



14.3 Quartic local action
A useful local quartic representative has the schematic form


                                     𝐺4 (𝜙, 𝑋) = 𝑔(𝜙) + 𝐶(𝜙)𝑋,

supplemented, where required, by the corresponding G_5-derivative jet represented locally through
a function


                                            𝐺5,𝜙 = 𝐷(𝜙).

The core principal targets are chosen so that the tensor kinetic functions remain positive and
approach their regular central limits.
The numerical witness yields approximately


                                             min ℱ = 1,

and


                                     min 𝒢 = min ℋ ≃ 0.999381.

The scalar kinetic target remains positive over the finite radial grid.

                                      𝐾scalar,min ≃ 2.5 × 10−15 ,

but the proper central Einstein-frame kinetic coeﬀicient has the finite positive limit

15. The Inner Handover
Because the genuine-SVT lobe extends to


                                              𝑢 = 0.715

while the regular quartic-Horndeski core reaches outward to


                                              𝑢 = 0.71,

there is a finite radial overlap


                                          0.71 ≤ 𝑢 ≤ 0.715

or, equivalently,


                                    1.3986014 ≲ 𝑥 ≲ 1.4084507.

The scalar profile is strictly monotone across this interval. With the normalization r_s=1, the
reconstructed P5 profile gives


                          Ξ(0.71) ≃ 0.84835352,         Ξ(0.715) ≃ 0.86076347,

and


                                   min |𝜙′ | ≃ 1.24037,         𝜙 ≡ Ξ.

Thus \phi itself is a valid local patch coordinate throughout the handover.
The action-level gluing is performed with a flat C^\infty partition rather than with a compactly
extended quintic. Let

                                                    𝜙 − 𝜙𝑎
                                          𝑡(𝜙) =            .
                                                    𝜙𝑏 − 𝜙𝑎

For 0<t<1 define


                                                       𝑒−1/𝑡
                                      𝑠∞ (𝑡) =
                                                 𝑒−1/𝑡 + 𝑒−1/(1−𝑡)

and extend it by s_\infty=0 for t\le0 and s_\infty=1 for t\ge1. Every derivative of s_\infty
vanishes at both endpoints. Consequently all finite action jets return exactly to their pure-sector


                                                  132
                                                   49
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                    Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


values at the patch boundaries.
This stronger endpoint property matters here. The full even-parity reducer contains radial deriv-
atives of coeﬀicient combinations that themselves depend on second and higher action derivatives.
A partition that is only C^2 is therefore suﬀicient as a geometric interpolation, but not the clean-
est choice for a finite-order same-action jet construction. The C^\infty partition removes this
bookkeeping ambiguity completely.
Both endpoint theories are locally healthy before gluing. The handover problem is therefore not one
of curing an instability, but of constructing a single smooth covariant action section that preserves
the fixed P5 background and the strict perturbative inequalities through the overlap.

16.2 What the historical SSZ bridge proves
The SSZ/JIF bridge corpus contains a mathematically useful but carefully limited result. If the
endpoint value, first derivative and second derivative are prescribed at both ends of a normalized
interval, then the functional

                                                      1
                                         𝐽3 [𝑠] = ∫ [𝑠‴ (𝑡)]2 𝑑𝑡
                                                  0


has the unique minimizer given by the quintic Hermite interpolant. For flat endpoint two-jets,


                    𝑠(0) = 0,    𝑠(1) = 1,      𝑠′ (0) = 𝑠′ (1) = 𝑠″ (0) = 𝑠″ (1) = 0,

this is


                                      𝑠𝐻5 (𝑡) = 10𝑡3 − 15𝑡4 + 6𝑡5 ,

with


                                             𝐽3 [𝑠𝐻5 ] = 720.

The variational proof follows from writing s=H_5+q with homogeneous endpoint two-jets:

                                                              1
                                𝐽3 [𝐻5 + 𝑞] − 𝐽3 [𝐻5 ] = ∫ (𝑞 ‴ )2 𝑑𝑡 ≥ 0.
                                                          0


This result is retained in the present work as a geometric and variational reference. It is not
used as a dynamical field equation. The older bridge audit correctly emphasizes that infinitely many
dynamical flows can share the same static radial profile.
There is also an important regularity distinction. When H_5 is extended by constants outside [0,1],


                                        𝐻5‴ (0) = 𝐻5‴ (1) = 60,

so the extended partition is C^2, not C^\infty. The full same-action construction below therefore
uses the flat C^\infty weight s_\infty; the quintic remains the unique minimum-J_3 member of
the fixed-two-jet class.

                                                    51

16.3 Scalar-coordinate same-action gluing
Because \phi=\Xi is monotone in both overlap bands, the transition can be written in field space
rather than by inserting the coordinate r explicitly into the fundamental action. For every inde-
pendent action function, introduce background-adapted normal coordinates n^A transverse to the
one-dimensional background curve in invariant space. A local function may be written as


                                          1              1
            𝐹 (𝜙, 𝑛) = 𝐹0 (𝜙) + 𝐴𝐴 (𝜙)𝑛𝐴 + 𝐻𝐴𝐵 (𝜙)𝑛𝐴 𝑛𝐵 + 𝑇𝐴𝐵𝐶 (𝜙)𝑛𝐴 𝑛𝐵 𝑛𝐶 + ⋯ .
                                          2              3!

Only a finite jet order is required by the quadratic perturbation problem. Multiplying the normal
expansion by a compact tubular cutoff away from the background curve gives an ordinary smooth
local action function rather than a collection of unrelated pointwise coeﬀicients.
The two endpoint representatives are glued at the level of the coeﬀicient functions F_0,A_A,H_{AB},\ldots
with s_\infty(\phi). Background-constrained coeﬀicients are then re-solved from


                          ℰ00 = 0,      ℰ11 = 0,          ℰ𝜙 = 0,    ℰ𝐴 = 0,

rather than blindly interpolated. Background-null transverse derivatives are retained as stability
controls.
This construction has three useful properties:
  1. it defines one smooth local covariant action on a tubular neighborhood of the P5 background
     curve;
  2. it recovers the pure representatives with all finite jets at the patch boundaries;
  3. it leaves precisely the transverse action derivatives that were already identified as background-
     null perturbative controls.
The handover is therefore an action-space continuation problem, not an interpolation of reduced
kinetic matrices.




17. Why Separately Reduced Matrices Must Not Be Added
Suppose one computes a reduced Maxwell–Horndeski even action

                                                  MH
                                                 𝑆red

after eliminating its nondynamical fields.
Suppose independently one computes

                                                  SVT
                                                 𝑆red .

                                   naive     MH     SVT    common
                                  𝑆hybrid = 𝑆red + 𝑆red − 𝑆red    .



                                                   135
                                                    52
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                                Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


This is not generally correct.
The reason is that the nondynamical fields are solved from algebraic or differential constraints whose
coeﬀicients depend on the full action.
If a nondynamical variable N appears schematically as


                                    𝐿 = 𝐴𝑁 2 + 𝑁 𝐵𝑖 𝑞𝑖̇ + 𝐶𝑖𝑗 𝑞𝑖̇ 𝑞𝑗̇ ,

                                                        𝐵𝑖 𝑞𝑖̇
                                               𝑁 =−            .
                                                        2𝐴

Substitution produces

                                                         𝐵𝑖 𝐵𝑗
                                      𝐿red = (𝐶𝑖𝑗 −            ) 𝑞𝑖̇ 𝑞𝑗̇ .
                                                          4𝐴

For two interactions,


                                  𝐴 = 𝐴 1 + 𝐴2 ,           𝐵 = 𝐵1 + 𝐵2 ,

and consequently

                                       (𝐵1 + 𝐵2 )2  𝐵2 𝐵2
                                                   ≠ 1 + 2
                                        𝐴1 + 𝐴 2    𝐴1  𝐴2

in general.
Therefore reduction and addition do not commute.
The hybrid theory must be assembled before eliminating the common constraints.

20. Jet-Aware Constraint Elimination
20.1 Why frozen-coeﬀicient Fourier reduction fails
An early common-kernel implementation froze all radial coeﬀicients at a point, replaced


                                     𝜕𝑡 → −𝑖𝜔,           𝜕𝑟 → 𝑖𝑘,

and then eliminated the nondynamical fields.
Although this procedure is often adequate for unconstrained systems at leading principal order, it
fails here.
The published reduced kinetic matrices contain radial derivatives such as


                                                 𝑎′4 ,

and derivatives of compound constraint quantities such as


                                               𝐾2′ .

These terms are produced during constraint elimination.
Freezing the coeﬀicient functions beforehand erases them.
Consequently,


                             freeze → Fourier transform → eliminate

does not reproduce the physical reduced principal operator.
The correct ordering is



combine radial action profiles → retain coeﬀicient jets → eliminate common constraints → take principal/eikona


This ordering is now adopted throughout the hybrid construction.

20.3 Pivot test
The denominator


                                                    𝐷ℎ1

could in principle vanish and produce a strong-coupling or constraint singularity.
We therefore evaluate it throughout the complete background-integrable SVT lobe.
For the lowest relevant gravitational multipole,


                                           ℓ = 2,         𝐿 = 6,

the minimum magnitude is


                                          min |𝐷ℎ1 | ≃ 11.99.

No sign change occurs.
The test was extended through a wide range of angular eigenvalues up to approximately


                                               𝐿 = 1000.

No zero of the pivot was found.
The absolute pivot grows rather than decreases with increasing L.
Consequently, the first common jet-aware elimination exhibits no constraint singularity in the light-
ring region.

                      (2)     1             𝑇           𝑇

characteristics with positive squared propagation speeds.
The relevance of the same-action theorem below is now precise. Away from the tested pivots, K, R,
G and the angular principal operator are smooth rational functions of a finite set of action jets.
Strict positivity of their healthy-sector margins is therefore an open condition in jet space. This is
what permits a controlled continuation through the two compact overlap bands.




21. Constructive Same-Action Closure
21.1 Meaning of “same-action closure”
The phrase same-action closure is used here in a precise local sense. It does not mean that the
data determine a unique fundamental Lagrangian on all of field space. It means that there exists
one smooth local covariant Horndeski–SVT action, defined on a tubular neighborhood of the one-
dimensional frozen P5 background trajectory, whose restrictions reproduce the already constructed
exterior Horndeski, strong-field genuine-SVT and inner quartic-Horndeski representatives and whose
quadratic principal symbol remains in the locally healthy domain through both handovers.
Write the common action schematically as



                                                     144
                                                      61
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                             Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16



                                    √
            𝑆HSVT [𝑔, 𝜙, 𝐴] = ∫ 𝑑4 𝑥 −𝑔 [ℒH (𝐺2 , 𝐺3 , 𝐺4 , 𝐺5 ) + ℒSVT (𝑓2 , 𝑓3 , 𝑓3̃ , 𝑓4 , 𝑓4̃ )],


with the shared Einstein/scalar/Maxwell pieces counted once according to the convention of Sec.
5.3. The closure problem is therefore a problem in the finite action-jet bundle sampled by the static
equations and the quadratic perturbation action.
At a point of the background curve let


                                                   𝒥 = (𝒥∥ , 𝒥⟂ )

denote the required finite collection of action values and derivatives. \mathcal J_{\parallel} con-
tains combinations constrained by the static field equations; \mathcal J_{\perp} contains trans-
verse derivatives that vanish from the background equations but enter the perturbation coeﬀicients.
The latter are the background-null controls.
The closure established below consists of four ingredients:
  1. a regular scalar coordinate \phi=\Xi on both overlap bands;
  2. a flat C^\infty partition in that scalar coordinate;
  3. nonsingular background/control Jacobians permitting the constrained and stability-sensitive
     jets to be re-solved smoothly;
  4. nonsingular common constraint pivots permitting the physical even operator to be formed
     throughout the continuation.
These four statements are enough to convert the previously separate healthy local representatives
into one local same-action section.

action.

                                                 𝑒−1/𝑡

This is the reason the compactly extended quintic Hermite bridge, although variationally optimal

21.3 Background re-solving rather than blind interpolation
A naive convex interpolation of all action coeﬀicients would not, by itself, guarantee the exact P5
background equations. The construction therefore interpolates only a provisional jet section and
then projects it back onto the background equation manifold.
Let


                                              𝒞𝐴 (𝜙, 𝒥) = 0

collect the independent static equations, for example


                          ℰ00 = 0,       ℰ11 = 0,         ℰ𝜙 = 0,        ℰ𝐴 = 0,

smoothly in the remaining freely specified jets Z, throughout a suﬀiciently small neighborhood. The
compact overlap interval is covered by finitely many such neighborhoods.
Operationally this is the procedure already used in the separate reconstructions: the P5 metric and
scalar profile are held fixed, freely selectable background-null data are specified, and the coeﬀicients
entering the actual background equations are re-solved. The handover therefore does not perturb
the frozen geometry merely to make the action convenient.

The control maps of Sec. 21.4 allow the background-null jets to be adjusted while the background-

jets to be solved locally so that


                                          T(𝒥(𝜙)) = T∗ (𝜙).

Because each handover interval is compact, the local implicit-function neighborhoods possess a finite
subcover. Continuing from one neighborhood to the next constructs a smooth healthy jet section
across the full band.
This argument is the step that turns “healthy endpoints” into a same-action local existence
result. It does not assume that arbitrary interpolation preserves stability; it actively re-solves the
available transverse controls to remain inside \mathscr U_+.



21.7 Realization as one local covariant action
A smooth jet section along the background curve still has to be realized by ordinary functions,
rather than being left as a table of pointwise derivatives. This is provided constructively by the
tubular expansion introduced in Sec. 16.3.
Let n^A be smooth normal coordinates transverse to the P5 trajectory in invariant space. For each
independent action function write, to the finite order needed by the quadratic problem,

                                           1              1
             𝐹 (𝜙, 𝑛) = 𝐹0 (𝜙) + 𝐴𝐴 (𝜙)𝑛𝐴 + 𝐻𝐴𝐵 (𝜙)𝑛𝐴 𝑛𝐵 + 𝑇𝐴𝐵𝐶 (𝜙)𝑛𝐴 𝑛𝐵 𝑛𝐶 + ⋯ .
                                           2              3!

The coeﬀicient functions are precisely the smooth same-action jet section constructed above. Multi-
plying the normal expansion by a smooth tubular cutoff that equals unity on the background curve
yields a genuine local smooth function of the covariant invariants. Repeating this for the finite set
of G_i, f_i and \tilde f_i functions produces one local covariant action, not a radius-by-radius
prescription.
Because s_\infty is flat at each patch boundary, the resulting action agrees with the selected pure
representatives to every finite derivative order used in the calculation. The exterior, strong-field
and core descriptions are therefore different coordinate patches/representatives of the same local
action construction rather than dynamically disconnected theories.
No uniqueness is implied. Background reconstruction is underdetermined, and the background-null
directions generate a family of admissible same-action completions.

                                                  67

21.8 Local Same-Action Closure Theorem
The preceding construction may be summarized as a theorem tailored to the frozen P5 background.
Theorem (constructive local same-action closure). Let the frozen P5 background satisfy the
source-locked geometry of Sec. 3, and let the exterior Horndeski, strong-field genuine-SVT and
inner quartic-Horndeski representatives be the locally healthy sections constructed in Secs. 6–14.
Assume on the two finite overlaps that:


                                𝑓 > 0,      ℎ > 0,       𝜙′ = Ξ′ ≠ 0;

that the background equation Jacobian for the chosen constrained jets is nonsingular; that the
Horndeski and SVT transverse control maps have full rank; and that the common nondynamical
pivots D_{h_1}, 2Lv_9 and \Delta_V do not vanish. Then there exists a C^\infty local covariant
Horndeski–SVT action on a tubular neighborhood of the complete P5 background trajectory such
that:


                                 ℰ𝜇𝜈 = 0,      ℰ𝜙 = 0,       ℰ𝐴 = 0

on the frozen background; the chosen pure representatives and all finite endpoint jets are recovered
outside the transition bands; the common even constraints are smoothly eliminable; and the local
odd/even principal symbol can be continued through both transitions without crossing a ghost,
Laplacian, hyperbolicity or constraint-singularity boundary.
Proof. Monotonicity of \phi supplies a scalar-coordinate chart on each overlap. The flat partition
s_\infty produces smooth provisional action jets and exact finite-jet endpoint recovery. The non-
singular background Jacobian re-solves the constrained jets so that the frozen P5 fields continue
to satisfy the static equations. The full-rank transverse control maps re-solve the background-null
jets so that the chosen stability targets follow a smooth path inside the strict healthy domain. The
nonzero constraint pivots make every Schur complement in the common even reduction smooth.
Strict positivity of the kinetic and characteristic margins is an open condition, and compactness of
each overlap gives a finite continuation cover. Finally, the tubular normal expansion realizes the
resulting smooth finite jet section as ordinary local action functions. QED.
The theorem is local in field space and along the static P5 branch. It is nevertheless the precise
closure needed to remove the previous “patchwork action” objection at the level addressed by the
present paper.

The construction was encoded in an independent machine-readable certificate with eight explicit
gates:

                                                 68

21.10 What is now closed, and what remains outside the closure claim
The following items are closed within the scope of this paper:
  • the P5 geometry is source-locked and independently reconstructible;
  • the exterior, light-ring and core action representatives overlap;
  • the transition is expressible in scalar field space with a flat C^\infty partition;
  • the background equations can be re-solved rather than violated by the handover;
  • the SVT transverse stability-control map has analytic rank three;
  • the full Horndeski principal-control map is nondegenerate on the certified interval;
  • h_1, H_1, \delta A_0, and \delta A_1 are algebraically eliminable with nonzero reference
    pivots;
  • the local odd/even principal-symbol health conditions can be continued through both overlaps;
  • the resulting smooth finite jet section can be realized by one local covariant action.
The following statements remain outside the closure theorem:
  • uniqueness or microscopic fundamentality of that action;
  • a single preferred closed-form choice for every free function over all field space;
  • an archived full K,R,G,M matrix dump for one arbitrarily selected member of the certified
    family;
  • the complete finite-\ell quasinormal spectrum and the lifetime of stable-trapped modes;


                                                152
                                                 69
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                     Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


   • nonlinear stability under finite perturbations;
   • proof that every effective characteristic cone lies inside the matter light cone;
   • a UV completion or a theorem excluding every nonperturbative pathology.
This distinction is essential. The local same-action closure is complete; the global dynamical
and microscopic interpretation of the resulting theory is not.
The internal SSZ Physics RAG was used as a provenance cross-check for the canonical \Xi hierarchy,
the historical variational bridge theorem, and the August dynamics-gap audit. The new September
P5 action-jet and closure-certificate numbers are treated as current project regressions rather than
retroactively labelled as RAG-independent validations.




22. Interpretation of the Large Characteristic Speeds
Several vector and angular characteristic speeds in the constructed SVT representative substantially
exceed unity.
It is important not to conflate three different statements.
A negative squared speed,


                                                𝑐2 < 0,

signals a Laplacian instability.
A zero speed can signal a degeneracy or strong-coupling issue depending on the surrounding kinetic
structure.
A positive super-unit speed,


                                                𝑐2 > 1,

does neither automatically.
The present result establishes only the first level of consistency:


                                                𝑐2 > 0.

A complete physical interpretation requires identifying the relevant causal cone of the interacting
gravitational–scalar–vector system and determining whether the effective theory admits a consistent
UV interpretation.
In modified-gravity systems, different modes need not propagate on the matter metric g_{\mu\nu}.
Their principal operators define effective metrics


                                                 𝒢𝜇𝜈
                                                  (𝑖)


whose null cones can differ.




                                                  153
                                                   70
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                      Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


Whether superluminal characteristics imply a fundamental inconsistency depends on additional
properties including global causal structure, analyticity, UV completion assumptions, and the oper-
ational definition of signal propagation.
We therefore make no claim of causal completion in this paper.
The appropriate terminology for the established result is:


                            local ghost/Laplacian/hyperbolicity stability.




23. Stable Trapping and the Difference Between Local and Global
Stability
The inner stable light ring deserves separate emphasis.
A healthy local principal symbol does not guarantee rapid decay of perturbations.
Stable trapping can prevent eﬀicient dispersal.
A wave packet concentrated near the stable null orbit may repeatedly return to the trapping region.
Even in a locally hyperbolic theory, this can produce slowly decaying resonances or unusually long-
lived quasinormal modes.
The shallow depth of the P5 null well,


                                            Δwell ≃ 0.42%,

may soften this effect, but it does not remove the underlying mathematical question.
The correct next observable calculation after local closure is therefore a finite-\ell spectral analysis.
One should compute the coupled tensor–scalar–vector quasinormal spectrum of the final hybrid
action and determine whether the stable orbit produces:


                                              Im 𝜔 → 0− ,

an actual growing mode


                                               Im 𝜔 > 0,

or merely a finite family of long-lived but decaying resonances.
This question cannot be answered from the eikonal local positivity tests alone.




24. Nonlinear Stability as a Separate Problem
Even a complete linear mode analysis would not prove nonlinear stability.



                                                  154
                                                   71
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                        Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


The full hybrid equations can schematically be written as


                                        ℰ𝐴 [Ψ, 𝜕Ψ, 𝜕 2 Ψ] = 0,

where \Psi collectively denotes


                                         𝑔𝜇𝜈 ,      𝜙,       𝐴𝜇 .

A nonlinear stability theorem would require an appropriate gauge choice, a hyperbolic formulation
of the evolution system, control of the constraint propagation, and energy estimates capable of
bounding suitable Sobolev norms of the perturbations.
The relevant logical implication would have the form


                        𝐸𝑁 (𝑡) < ∞     ⟹         the solution can be continued.

One would then seek estimates such as

                                         𝑑𝐸𝑁
                                             ≤ 𝐶(𝐸𝑁 )𝐸𝑁 .

A suﬀiciently strong bound could rule out finite-time blow-up for suﬀiciently small perturbations of
the P5 background.
Nothing in the present local principal-symbol analysis is presented as a substitute for such a theorem.
Rather, the present work establishes the prerequisite that the linearized local equations possess a
healthy characteristic structure in the first place.

25.2 P5 is not a regular black hole in the strict causal sense
The phrase “regular black hole” is often used for nonsingular metrics that preserve an event horizon
while replacing the Schwarzschild singularity by a regular core, frequently a de Sitter-like region.
Many such models possess both an outer event horizon and an inner Cauchy horizon; the latter
raises the separate mass-inflation stability problem.
The frozen P5 closure belongs to a different causal class. The global scan gives

                                                  1
                                                𝑓≥ ,
                                                  4

and


                                           ℎ ≥ 0.3268566652

through the source-locked domain and its regular-core continuation. There is no zero of either
metric function and hence no event horizon or inner Cauchy horizon of the usual static spherical
type.
P5 therefore avoids the standard inner-horizon mass-inflation problem by not possessing that horizon
structure in the first place. This should not be advertised as a generic stability advantage over
regular black holes, because P5 acquires a different potential problem: stable trapping at the inner
light ring.



25.3 Relation to horizonless ultracompact objects
A horizonless object with a light ring belongs naturally to the ultracompact-object class. In broad
metric theories, light-ring structure is strongly constrained topologically; under additional Einstein-
equation and energy-condition assumptions, smooth horizonless ultracompact objects are known to
develop light rings in pairs with a stable member.
P5 exhibits precisely the geometric pair


                                              𝑥out = 1.5

and


                                           𝑥in ≃ 1.41616064.

One should nevertheless distinguish the general geometric pairing argument from the GR-specific
energy-condition theorem. The final P5 support is a Horndeski–SVT effective stress system, so
conclusions relying specifically on the Einstein equations with ordinary matter cannot simply be
imported unchanged.
The useful comparison is therefore structural rather than identificational: P5 occupies the horizon-
less ultracompact sector, but with a covariantly reconstructed scalar–vector–tensor support rather
than a prescribed GR material star.




                                                 157
                                                  74
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                    Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


25.4 Stable trapping: the principal unresolved physical risk
Stable trapping is the strongest reason not to equate local hyperbolicity with global viability.
For wave equations on spacetimes with stable trapping, rigorous results show that uniform decay
can be no faster than logarithmic in relevant classes. In ultracompact-object models the stable null
orbit is also associated with very long-lived modes, and nonlinear numerical evolutions of horizonless
bosonic stars have exhibited light-ring-triggered migration or collapse.
The P5 case has an important quantitative peculiarity: its null well is shallow,


                                        Δwell ≃ 4.18 × 10−3 .

Nonlinear ultracompact-object studies indicate that very shallow stable-light-ring wells can substan-
tially lengthen the instability timescale. That is encouraging but not exculpatory. It means the
physically decisive calculation is now a timescale calculation, not another local sign test.
After same-action closure, the next required program is therefore:


            finite-ℓ spectrum → quasinormal/resonant lifetimes → nonlinear evolution.

A viable outcome would be either the absence of growing modes with suﬀiciently rapid decay,
or trapping timescales so long that the configuration remains astrophysically relevant. A rapidly
growing finite-\ell or nonlinear mode would instead falsify the proposed stationary P5 compact
object even though all local ghost and Laplacian conditions are positive.



25.5 Relation to the SSZ interpretation
The covariant reconstruction should also be distinguished from the conceptual motivation of Seg-
mented Spacetime.
SSZ begins from a geometrical/operational interpretation in which phenomena conventionally en-
coded as spacetime curvature are organized through a primary segmentation variable \Xi, with


                                           𝐷 = (1 + Ξ)−1 .

The present P5 calculation strengthens that connection: the temporal metric remains exactly \Xi-
primary, while the radial geometry is generated by the susceptibility closure \chi(\Xi) rather than
by an independent fitted metric function.
At the same time, the paper deliberately does not require the reader to accept a microscopic
interpretation of segmentation. The covariant question is narrower:
     Can the source-locked P5 geometry be embedded into a local field theory whose back-
     ground equations and perturbative principal symbol are healthy?
This separation is useful because it leaves three logically independent layers:
  1. the SSZ interpretation of \Xi;
  2. the P5 geometrical closure \Xi\to(f,h);
  3. the Horndeski–SVT dynamical realization of that geometry.


                                                 158
                                                  75
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                    Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


Agreement or failure at one layer need not be disguised as agreement or failure at another. In
particular, the old SSZ Hermite bridge remains a mathematically justified interpolation template,
while the present Horndeski–SVT action is the object that must ultimately determine the physical
dynamics.




26. Main Results
The analysis produces a closed local dynamical embedding of the frozen P5 geometry.
The geometry remains fixed throughout. Its outer unstable light ring is


                                              𝑥 = 1.5,

and its stable inner ring is


                                          𝑥 ≃ 1.41616064.

The outer orbit reproduces the Schwarzschild temporal/radial metric values and null-potential cur-
vature at the calibrated point, while the inner ring generates a shallow stable-trapping well. The
global P5 continuation remains horizonless with positive f and h.
The exterior admits a finite Horndeski scalar carrier with positive kinetic margin. The complete
two-light-ring domain admits a background-integrable genuine U(1) SVT action section. Its scalar
equation is solved explicitly through the reconstruction of f_{3,X}. The odd sector has positive
kinetic and gradient margins; the corrected even kinetic quantity obeys


                                           𝒦 ≥ 10.5415,

and the independent e_1 identity is satisfied at the 10^{-10} level. The scalar radial characteristic
remains near unity,

                                                 2

while the tensor, vector and coupled angular squared characteristics remain positive. The regular
quartic-Horndeski core overlaps the genuine-SVT lobe.
The action-level handover is constructed in the monotone scalar coordinate \phi=\Xi with a
flat C^\infty partition. Background-constrained jets are re-solved from the static equations;
background-null transverse jets are used to control the perturbative principal symbol. The selected
SVT control map has the analytic determinant

                                                  1
                                        det MSVT = ℎ𝜙′2 ,

which is strictly nonzero on both overlap bands.
The common even-parity reduction is also complete at the algebraic level. The generalized variable

Combining the nonzero background/control ranks, the strict pure-sector margins, the smooth con-

                PASS_CONSTRUCTIVE_LOCAL_SAME_ACTION_CLOSURE_CERTIFICATE.

The principal result is therefore stronger than the statement that several healthy patches exist: one
smooth local covariant Horndeski–SVT action can be constructed through the full P5
background trajectory, including both light rings and both action handovers, without
a forced local ghost, Laplacian, hyperbolicity or constraint singularity.




27. What Is Not Proven by the Closure
The local closure theorem deliberately stops short of several stronger claims.
It does not select a unique fundamental action. The inverse reconstruction is underdetermined, and
the background-null directions generate a family of same-action completions.
It does not provide one privileged elementary closed-form expression for every free G_i, f_i, and
\tilde f_i over all of invariant field space. The theorem constructs a smooth local action in a


                                                160
                                                 77
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                    Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


tubular neighborhood of the P5 trajectory, which is the domain needed for the background and its
local perturbations.
It does not replace an implementation-level archive of the complete reduced K,R,G,M matrices for a
chosen representative. Producing that matrix dump and rerunning endpoint/pure-limit regressions
remains valuable for independent reproducibility, but it is no longer an existence obstruction.
It does not establish that every positive effective characteristic speed is subluminal relative to the
matter cone. In fact several vector/angular characteristics are substantially super-unit in the chosen
normalization, so causal-cone and EFT interpretation remain separate questions.
It does not compute the complete finite-\ell quasinormal spectrum. Therefore it does not determine
the lifetime or nonlinear significance of the shallow stable-trapping well.
It does not prove nonlinear stability, global well-posedness for arbitrary finite data, the absence of
every nonperturbative pathology, or a UV completion.
The post-closure distinction is therefore


              local same-action existence and principal-symbol regularity: CLOSED

versus


              global spectral, nonlinear, causal and microscopic completion: OPEN.




28. Post-Closure Program
The next calculation is no longer another attempt to make the Horndeski and SVT patches meet.
They already meet at the level of the local action and principal symbol.
The first post-closure task is representative-level reproducibility: select one explicit member of
the certified same-action family, generate the complete radial arrays for the unreduced coeﬀicients
and the reduced K,R,G,M matrices, and archive the full pure-Horndeski, pure-SVT and transition
regressions. This will turn the existence certificate into a convenient benchmark implementation.
The second task is the finite-multipole spectral problem. The coupled perturbation equations should
be solved with physically appropriate boundary conditions across the full horizonless geometry,
yielding the quasinormal and trapped-mode spectrum. The stable inner light ring is the central
target, because a positive local principal symbol does not determine the lifetime of modes trapped
in a shallow potential well.
The third task is nonlinear evolution. Time-domain simulations should test whether the stable-
trapping region drives migration, collapse, long-lived oscillations, or remains benign on astrophysi-
cally relevant timescales.
The fourth task is the causal/EFT interpretation of the characteristic cones. The effective scalar,
vector and tensor cones should be compared with the matter light cone, and the large positive
vector/angular speeds should be analyzed in terms of hyperbolicity, front velocity, cutoff scale and
possible UV completion rather than being conflated with a Laplacian instability.
Finally, rotation should be incorporated. A slowly rotating extension would test whether the two-
light-ring structure and the same-action control rank persist once spherical symmetry is relaxed,

                                                 161
                                                  78
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                   Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


before attempting a fully stationary axisymmetric construction.




29. Conclusion
We investigated the dynamical realizability of the frozen P5 Segmented-Spacetime geometry within
a covariant Horndeski–scalar–vector–tensor framework while refusing to alter the geometry merely
to fit a convenient theory.
The P5 background is \Xi-primary in its temporal sector and is closed radially by the source-locked
susceptibility relation. It is horizonless and regular, contains an outer unstable light ring at


                                             𝑥 = 1.5,

and an inner stable light ring at


                                         𝑥 ≃ 1.41616064.

A finite exterior Horndeski carrier, a background-integrable genuine-SVT light-ring carrier and a
regular quartic-Horndeski core were constructed with overlapping radial domains. The genuine-SVT
lobe passes the employed local odd/even ghost and Laplacian tests with strict margins.
The central closure problem was then treated at the unreduced action level. The common
generalized variable

                                               𝐿𝑎4     𝑎
                                    𝜓 = 𝐻2 +       ℎ1 + 1 𝛿𝜙′
                                               𝑎3      𝑎3

provides the shared constraint coordinate. The h_1 constraint is regular, and the remaining H_1,
\delta A_0, and \delta A_1 variables possess exact nonsingular elimination formulas. In particu-
lar,


                                          Δ𝑉 = −4𝐶odd

in the pure genuine-SVT limit, so the already positive odd-sector determinant supplies an analytic
lower bound on the even constraint pivot.
The overlap itself is implemented in scalar field space with a flat C^\infty partition. The frozen
background equations are re-solved during the continuation rather than violated by naive interpola-
tion. Background-null transverse action derivatives supply the required perturbative controls; the
selected SVT control submap has

                                                1
                                      det MSVT = ℎ𝜙′2 ≠ 0
                                                2

on both handovers. Since the common reduced operator is smooth away from the tested pivots
and all endpoint stability margins are strict, the transverse controls can be continued through the
compact overlap bands while remaining inside the healthy principal-symbol domain. A tubular
invariant-space expansion then realizes those smooth jets as one ordinary local covariant action.


                                               162
                                                79
SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                      Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16


This establishes the result that was previously missing:


        For the frozen P5 background there exists a smooth local covariant same-action
        Horndeski–SVT realization across the exterior, both strong-field light rings, and the
        regular core. The static background equations can be satisfied throughout both han-
        dovers, every nondynamical even-parity pivot exposed by the common reduction re-
        mains nonzero on the certified reference construction, and the local odd/even principal
        symbol can be continued without a forced ghost, Laplacian, hyperbolicity or constraint
        singularity. The local same-action closure is therefore complete.

This conclusion is deliberately local and constructive. It does not identify a unique microscopic
theory, and it does not turn local hyperbolicity into a global stability theorem. The scientifically
important open questions now begin after closure: finite-\ell quasinormal modes and stable trap-
ping, nonlinear dynamics, effective causal cones, rotation and UV completion.

Appendix C. Literature Framework and Provenance Boundaries
The full \mathcal L_2–\mathcal L_5 Horndeski definitions used in Sec. 5 follow the generalized-
Galileon representation of Kobayashi, Yamaguchi and Yokoyama, which is equivalent to Horndeski
theory up to the standard mappings and integrations by parts.
The explicit second-order U(1)-gauge-invariant scalar–vector–tensor interactions f_2, f_3, \tilde
f_3, f_4, and \tilde f_4 follow the gauge-invariant SVT construction used by Heisenberg and
Tsujikawa. The odd-parity general-SVT stability framework is associated with Heisenberg, Kase
and Tsujikawa, while the even-parity general-SVT conditions used for the P5 lobe follow Zhang and
Kase.
The Maxwell–Horndeski pure-limit regression follows Kase and Tsujikawa. Their \ell\ge2 decom-
position contains two physical odd modes and three physical even modes, matching the mode count
used in the common hybrid reducer.
The stable-trapping discussion is intentionally separated from the local ghost/Laplacian result. Keir
establishes logarithmic limitations on uniform wave decay in relevant stably trapped spherical geome-
tries. Cardoso et al. connect stable null orbits in ultracompact objects with long-lived modes and
possible nonlinear problems. Cunha, Berti and Herdeiro establish strong light-ring pairing results
for smooth ultracompact objects, with the GR+NEC assumptions carefully distinguished from the
more general topological part. Fully nonlinear bosonic-star evolutions by Cunha et al. show light-
ring-triggered migration or collapse and also emphasize that very shallow wells can lengthen the
relevant timescale.
The comparison with regular black holes follows the review by Bonanno and Saueressig: many
standard regular-black-hole geometries retain an outer event horizon and an inner Cauchy horizon.
Since the frozen P5 closure has f>0 and h>0 everywhere in the tested global continuation, it belongs
instead to the horizonless ultracompact class.
The SSZ-specific provenance is deliberately kept separate from established external theory. The
canonical SSZ baseline \Xi_{\rm weak}, \Xi_{\rm strong}, D and the historical C^2 transition are
documented in the SSZ complete-documentation corpus, with SSZ_CANONICAL_FORMULAS_2026.md
used as the later source hierarchy where older branch-lock files conflict. The fixed-two-jet variational
theorem is documented in SSZ_Variational_Bridge_Paper_Wrede_Casu_BingsiAI_v2.pdf;
the warning that a frozen bridge does not uniquely determine dynamics is documented in
SSZ_Middle_Bridge_Dynamics_Gap_Audit_2026-08-29.md. Neither internal source is treated as
external validation of P5. They provide model provenance, a tested interpolation theorem, and a
clear separation between geometry and dynamics.

2. Terminology: what exactly was meant by "closed"
The project accumulated several distinct closure layers. Keeping them separate is essential for later work.

2.1 Slot closure: 41/41
The common unreduced even-parity coefficient language contains exactly 41 coefficient slots:
                    a1...a9 (9) + b1...b5 (5) + c1...c6 (6) + d1...d4 (4) + e1...e4 (4) + v1...v13 (13) = 41.
"41/41 closed" means that complete unreduced coefficient tables exist for selected local/sector representatives in this common
basis. It does not by itself prove that all radial sectors belong to one identical holonomic action member, nor that the reduced
finite-l kinetic matrix is positive everywhere.




                                                                                                                                        Page 3


                                                                          177
      SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                                         Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16
SSZ P5 / Horndeski-SVT finite-l closure - technical memory - 16.09.2026



2.2 Constructive same-action closure
This is an existence statement. The outer and inner overlap bands possess enough background-constrained and background-
null action-jet freedom to connect the local representatives by smooth C-infinity handovers while retaining the frozen P5
geometry and nonzero common constraint pivots.

2.3 Principal global closure
A separate center-to-infinity characteristic/principal stream exists. It proves that one can select a globally healthy principal-
symbol representation. It is not automatically the same basis, same lower-order member, or same action representative as an
independently built finite-l mass/potential block.

2.4 Finite-l global same-action closure
This is the strongest level required before a coupled QNM spectrum can be called physical: one holonomic center-to-infinity
action member, with both handovers directly evaluated from that member, reduced with all profile derivatives retained, and
passing K>0 plus radial and angular gradient gates at finite l. This final level is not yet passed.

3. Frozen P5 geometry and theory architecture
3.1 Background variables
The P5 geometry is source-locked in the segment variable Xi. With r_s=1, C=r_s/r=1/x and phi=Xi(C). The susceptibility
chi=dXi/dC obeys the autonomous P5 closure dXi/dC=(1-Xi)^(3/2) P5(Xi). The temporal metric is f=(1+Xi)^(-2); h is fixed by the
P5 radial closure.
      Outer unstable light ring: C=2/3, x=1.5.
      Inner stable light ring: C approximately 0.706135, x approximately 1.41616064.
      Regular horizonless center.
The historical Hermite middle bridge is provenance only and is not the P5 field equation.

3.2 Exact kappa identity and the 00/11 basis issue
                                       phi_r = -C^2 chi,       kappa = h phi_r^2 = h C^4 chi^2 = -2 X.
For the metric background block (f2,f2X), the E00/E11 Jacobian has the triangular form J=[[-A,0],[-A,-B]] with A=r^2 f and B=A
kappa. Merely swapping E00 and E11 cannot improve singular values. The algebraically natural basis is instead E0=E00 and
EDelta=E11-E00, in which the Jacobian is diagonal: diag(-A,-A kappa).
This is the precise version of the earlier 00/11 rotation/inversion intuition: the useful operation is not a blind row swap but a
regime-aware basis change. The same kappa=-2X also controls the rank of the simple SVT Hessian control map. Near the exact
center kappa->0, so the scalar coordinate chart must be replaced by the separate regular Taylor-center branch rather than
divided through by kappa.

3.3 Hybrid covariant architecture
                                                 C_HSVT = C_MH + ( C_SVT - C_shared ).
This addition must be performed on the common unreduced quadratic action before eliminating H0,H1,H2,h1,dA0,dA1 or the
auxiliary vector variable V. Constraint elimination is nonlinear in the coefficient set, so Reduce(A+B) is not equal to Reduce(A)
+Reduce(B) in general.
The physical architecture is therefore Horndeski exterior -> genuine U(1)-SVT light-ring lobe -> Horndeski core, with the genuine-
SVT lobe containing both light rings.

characteristic minima include:

5. Same-action handovers and background re-solving
5.1 Outer handover
The explicit re-solved outer background band covers approximately u=0.551523087 to 0.61. The flat partition is genuinely flat at
the Horndeski endpoint: S_SVT=0 through the initial plateau, rises across the overlap, and reaches S_SVT=1 at u=0.61. Typical
values are approximately S=0.001 near u=0.575, S=0.065 near 0.58, S=0.501 near 0.59, S=0.935 near 0.60, and 1 at 0.61.

3.2e-16, max |J_A| about 1.6e-15.

5.2 Inner handover
The explicit inner band covers u=0.71...0.715. The actual re-solved residuals are machine zero (E00 about 8.9e-16, E11 about
4.7e-15, J_A about 3.1e-15). A previous diagnostic column E_H+E_SVTg is not the complete residual in this band because
partition derivatives and re-solved lower Horndeski jets contribute. Treating that naive sum as a failure was superseded.
The stored genuine-SVT lower values themselves visibly follow the action partition: f2=S_SVT*f2_full and f2X=S_SVT*f2X_full to
numerical precision, with f2F approximately S_SVT. This is why a split block must not be reinterpreted as an unsplit full-SVT
background action.

6. Background-null controls and why local existence survived the handovers
The static background fixes only action data tangent to the one-dimensional background trajectory. Transverse jets remain
available for perturbative control. For the genuine-SVT Hessian controls H=(HXX,HXF,HXY,HFF,HFY,HYY), the three radial
channels are:
    Delta v1 = HFF - 4 kappa HFY + 4 kappa^2 HYY
    Delta v4 = -HXF + 2 kappa HXY - 4 Fbg HFY + 8 Fbg kappa HYY
    Delta c2 = (kappa/2) HXX - Fbg HXF + 6 Fbg kappa HXY - 4 Fbg^2 HFY + 16 Fbg^2 kappa HYY
In the selector gauge HXY=HFY=HYY=0, the exact right inverse is:
                           HFF = Delta v1,       HXF = -Delta v4,         HXX = (2/kappa)(Delta c2 - Fbg Delta v4).
                                                              det M_rad = kappa/2.
Certified kappa minima are approximately 0.098838 in the outer overlap, 0.207862 in the central lobe, and 0.632753 in the inner
overlap, so this control map does not lose rank there. Independent unused Hessian directions remain available for angular-
channel regulation. The Horndeski principal-control map with targets (F,H,a1,c4,c2) was likewise found full rank through the
onset-to-core interval.

7. Radial derivative service, holonomicity, and branch corrections
7.1 JET9D8
The accepted radial derivative service is a local polynomial jet on the nonuniform r=x grid with window=9 and polynomial
degree=8. This replaced spline-based derivative experiments for production finite-l work.
    v12=-v6/(2h) regression: absolute residual around 1e-14 or better.
    v13: median scaled relative error around 6e-6 on the central regression; edge maxima are derivative/rounding sensitive.
    e4: isolated median at the few-1e-7 scale; end-to-end regression is also strong.
The previously selected plus branch v12=+v6/(2h) is superseded. Pure Horndeski has v6=0, so the sign was invisible there, but
it matters in genuine-SVT and hybrid bands.

7.2 Correct a5
                                                   a5 = a2' - a1'' - (A0' v4 / 2)' + A0' v5 / 2.
For pure Horndeski A0'=0, this reduces to a5=a2'-a1''. A previous builder omitted -a1'' and is not authoritative.

7.3 Holonomic on-curve partials
Two Appendix expressions exposed the same practical point: in the selected one-dimensional action representative, partial
derivatives appearing in d3 are represented by the holonomic on-curve derivatives, e.g. partial_phi v6=(dv6/dr)/phi_r and
partial_phi a4=a4'/phi_r. Using naive explicit partials generated false mismatches. With the holonomic convention, d3 regression
closes to numerical/JET accuracy.

7.4 Central action holonomicity
Direct chain-rule checks such as df3/dr=f3_phi phi_r+f3_X X_r and the corresponding relations for f3_X, f4, f4_X, f4_XX, f2 and
f2_X close in the interior, usually around 1e-6 or better and much tighter for several upper jets. Therefore the temporary
hypothesis that the central action data were fundamentally nonholonomic was rejected.

9. Complete common even-parity constraint elimination
Before reduction the common gauge-fixed fields are (H0,H1,H2,h1,dphi,dA0,dA1,V). The physical basis is chi=(psi,dphi,V)^T
with
                                                   psi = H2 + (L a4/a3) h1 + (a1/a3) dphi' .
Define p=a1/a3, q=L a4/a3 and
   B    = a2 - v2 v4/(2 v1)
   Cphi = a5 + L a6 - v2 v5/(2 v1)
   CH2 = a7 + L a8 - v2 v3/(2 v1)


                                                                                                                            Page 7


                                                                          181
     SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                                   Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16
SSZ P5 / Horndeski-SVT finite-l closure - technical memory - 16.09.2026


     Ch1 = L (a9 - v2 v6/(2 v1))
     Beff = B - a3 p' - CH2 p
     Dh1 = Ch1 - a3 q' - CH2 q
                                     h1 = -(a3 psi' + Beff dphi' + Cphi dphi + CH2 psi + v2 V) / Dh1.
The remaining two algebraic time-derivative constraints use DeltaV=4 b1 v10-v11^2. Finally dA0=(v1 V)'/(L v9) -(v8 h1+v12
H2+v13 dphi)/(2v9). The full JET9D8 implementation exports linear profile maps for h1,H2,H1,dA1,dA0 in y=(psi,dphi,V) and y'.
On the central 4000-point reference, L=6 gives min |Dh1|=11.987940, min |DeltaV|=3.042884 and min |2 L v9|=0.119951. The
dphi'' and h1' higher-derivative cancellations close to zero / about 1e-16.

10. Profile-aware reducer and the missing S block
The decisive structural correction on Sep-16 was to retain the antisymmetric first-order radial block. Zhang-Kase Eq. 4.25 has
the reduced form
                           L_even^(2) = dot(Y)^T K dot(Y) + Y'^T G Y' + Y'^T S Y + Y^T M Y,             S^T=-S.
In the project convention used by the profile reducer, after applying the formal differential transformation T and integrating by
parts:
     K = -P20/2
     G = +P02/2        (project action uses -chi'^T G chi')
     R = -P11/2 = 0
     S = 1/2 (P02' - P01)
     M = -1/2 (P00 + S')
Earlier attempts to force the antisymmetric first-order radial contribution into M caused an apparent lower-order mismatch. Once
S was retained explicitly, the reducer high-L mass regression passed.

10.1 Structural reducer audit
On the central reference there are no differential operator terms above second radial order, the H0 quadratic residual is about
4.55e-13, and the internal principal K comparison is typically at 1e-12 scale or better. The project has R=0 in the final profile
convention.

11. High-L mass/potential closure
The direct-action high-L mass formulas serve as an independent regression target for the profile reducer. The corrected leading
terms are:
     M11 = -r^2 v6^2/(4 v1)
     M13 = -r v6/2
     M33 = -v1
     M22 = e4 + 2 h c4 a6/a4 - m5_minus^2/(4 a4^2 v9)
     z1       = (a4 + r a9 - 0.5 r A0prime v6) v6
     m1_plus = +r a4 v8 + z1           # c4 sector
     m1_minus = -r a4 v8 + z1          # dA0/v9 Schur sector
     m2       = 2 c5 v1 + (A0prime v1 + 0.5 phiprime v4) v6
     m3_plus = a4 v1' + 0.5 A0prime v1 v6
     m4       = 2 A0prime v1 + phiprime v4
     m5_minus = a4 v13 - a6 v6
     M12 = -r d3/2 - h c4 z1/(a4 v6) - r h a6 m2/(2 a4 v1) + r v5 v6/(4 v1)
           - m1_minus m5_minus/(4 a4^2 v9)
           + (1/4) d/dr [2 r h c4 + r(2 d2 v1-v4 v6)/(2 v1) + r v6 m5_minus/(2 a4 v9)]

     M23 = h A0prime c4 v1/a4 + v5/2 + m3_plus m5_minus/(2 a4^2 v9)
           - h a6 m4/(2 a4) - (1/4) d/dr [v4 + v1 m5_minus/(a4 v9)]
The sign in m5_minus and the distinction m1_plus versus m1_minus are essential. Spline-based mass/angular scans that
violated these identities were superseded.

12. Why the old 41/41 tables could not simply be concatenated
A diagnostic global 41-stream concatenation was intentionally attempted and rejected. It exposed two separate issues:
1.    The archived outer 37/41 table was a repaired pure-SVT branch, not the complete same-action Horndeski+SVT handover.
      Its coefficients became nonzero before the stored S_SVT partition actually turned on.

2.     At u=0.61, the re-solved outer repaired-SVT endpoint and the Sep-13 central exact SVT table are different action-jet
       representatives on the same background. Their upper/background values f3,f4,N4 are close, but f3X, f2, f2X and Hessian
       jets are drastically different.
This is not a contradiction: background-constrained jets and background-null transverse jets are not unique. It does mean that a
direct coefficient concatenation is not a proof of one same action.

13. Sep-16 outer finite-l endgame - what was tried and what each attempt taught
us
13.1 First simple cubic/Einstein-like MH member
A deliberately simple G4=1/2, G5=0 Maxwell-Horndeski member was combined with the genuine-SVT Delta-C block. Its
structural reduction was finite, but K became negative. Large scans of the genuine-SVT Hessian controls, including Delta v1
variations far beyond the initial value, barely moved the bad kinetic direction. The problematic eigenvector was nearly scalar.
Therefore the failure was not fixable by simply turning the radial f2 Hessian knobs.

13.2 Control experiment with the archived strong-H carrier
The same genuine-SVT Delta-C contribution combined at coefficient level with the already healthy strong-H principal carrier gave
a high-L K with no negative rows in the diagnostic L=1000 run (minimum eigenvalue approximately 1.18e-6). This isolated the
missing freedom to the Horndeski principal member, not the ZK emitter or the reducer.

13.3 Recovery of the actual strong-H action data
The archived file ssz_p5_horndeski_carrier_through_light_rings_to_core_2026-09-12.csv was recovered. It directly stores the
variable G4(phi), G4_phi and G3_X principal carrier. The strong-H table has F=G=H and a positive target scalar kinetic margin
about 9.7423e-3. This was the correct input for a variable-G4(phi), G5=0 MH emitter.

13.4 Auxiliary-V recanonicalization after hybrid assembly
A major algebraic trap was discovered: adding two coefficient sets that had each already been auxiliary-V completed does not
preserve the H0^2 identity. After summing the common unreduced blocks, the shared auxiliary V sector must be canonicalized
once again. The required identity is v7=v2^2/(4v1) in the corresponding canonical route. A pre-recanonicalization residual around
0.684 was therefore an assembly error, not physics. After common recanonicalization, the residual is exactly zero in the final
audit.

13.5 Final variable-G4 outer candidate - important on-shell caveat
The final outer candidate used:



                                                                                                                                 Page 9

         SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                                    Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16
SSZ P5 / Horndeski-SVT finite-l closure - technical memory - 16.09.2026


         the variable-G4(phi), G5=0 healthy MH principal carrier reconstructed from the archived strong-H action data;
         the re-solved outer geometry and A0' profile;
         the genuine-SVT Delta-C block assembled before constraint elimination;
         common auxiliary-V recanonicalization after assembly;
         v12=-v6/(2h);
         selected lower-order member v5=c3=e3=0;
         a5 regenerated holonomically with JET9D8.
All 41 slots are finite on the 2201-point outer grid, and several assembly identities close. However, this construction is not a strict
certified same-action/on-shell member: the variable-G4 strong-H principal data were interpolated onto the already re-solved
outer background that had been constructed for a different Horndeski representative, and the full background equations were not
re-solved after that substitution. The file names containing FINAL_SAME_ACTION therefore overstate the status and should be
read as historical run names. The candidate is nevertheless rejected because the symmetric kinetic quadratic form is negative
over a large radial set.

14. Final variable-G4 candidate operator audit - decisive for the candidate, not a
theory no-go
For this candidate, the symmetric part of the 3x3 kinetic quadratic form is not positive definite. The audit code explicitly
diagonalizes Ks=(K+K^T)/2. This is the correct quadratic-form test even when a small numerical antisymmetric remainder is
present. The detailed audit across L is:

                                                                                                       max S sym
    L                    min eig(K)         negative rows         min radial c^2   negative c^2 rows                      max |R|
                                                                                                       residual


    6                    -2.116849e+03      2113                  8.882930e-02     0                   2.53e-08           0.0e+00

    12                   -6.688485e+02      2065                  9.061206e-02     0                   2.08e-08           0.0e+00

    20                   -2.363717e+02      2025                  6.387482e-02     0                   1.51e-08           0.0e+00

    1000                 -1.064799e-01      629                   1.075190e-04     0                   1.71e-08           0.0e+00

Additional checks: the H0 auxiliary identity and v12 identity close exactly, no forbidden higher-order operator keys appear, R=0,
G is symmetric to floating-point accuracy, and S is antisymmetric at about 1e-8. The raw time-principal block P20/K, however,
retains a nonzero antisymmetric numerical remainder in this transplanted candidate (for the stored L=6 and L=42 profiles the
relative max asymmetry is about 1.6e-4; at L=1000 about 1.2e-6). This does not affect the quadratic form after symmetrization,
but it is another reason not to label the candidate a fully clean final member. Constraint pivots remain nonzero.

14.1 Spatial location of the kinetic failure
Using the stored L=6,42,1000 matrix profiles, the negative region begins progressively farther inward as L increases:

    L                                              negative-K interval on stored grid           location of most negative point

    6                                              u about 0.552613 to 0.610000                 minimum near u=0.608139

    42                                             u about 0.554474 to 0.610000                 minimum near u=0.608139

    1000                                           u about 0.566913 to 0.600830                 minimum near u=0.583366
The high-L weakening explains why principal-only or asymptotic diagnostics can look healthy while finite-l remains unacceptable.
It also shows why QNM must wait for an explicitly finite-l healthy member.

14.2 What the failure does and does not mean
    Does mean
    The Sep-16 variable-G4 transplant candidate must be rejected. Its symmetrized kinetic quadratic form has a negative
    direction over a large part of the outer band, and it was not independently re-solved on the background after changing the
    Horndeski principal member.

SSZ P5 / Horndeski-SVT finite-l closure - technical memory - 16.09.2026



  Does not mean
  It does not show that a certified global same-action finite-l member has failed, because this candidate was not fully
  background re-solved after the variable-G4 substitution. It also does not invalidate the P5 geometry, local strong-H witness,
  central genuine-SVT light-ring witness, constructive same-action existence theorem, 41/41 coefficient completion, derivative
  service, constraint maps, ZK emitter, MH emitter, or profile reducer.



15. QNM status and why calculation stops here
A coupled QNM spectrum is not scientifically meaningful at this stage. The last candidate is rejected and no certified center-to-
infinity on-shell finite-l member has yet passed the gates. The QNM gate is therefore deliberately closed.
Independent work on the asymptotic exterior remains useful: an outgoing Jost expansion was transported with order-10 error
around 2.43e-12. The previously attempted inward shooting of damped modes on the real radial axis was rejected because the
growing complementary solution contaminates the integration and candidate roots moved when the outer radius changed.
Acceptable production approaches after a healthy global KRGM member exists include exterior complex scaling, compactified
Jost/spectral determinants, or a continued-fraction-type formulation. Any eventual root must be tested for radial resolution,
domain/outer-boundary, complex-scaling angle, basis normalization, branch tracking and determinant convergence.

17. Correct future continuation - one targeted control problem, not another
tuning loop
If the project is resumed, the next task should not be another hand-picked representative. The correct problem is to formulate the
remaining Horndeski/SVT transverse jet freedom as a constrained continuation/optimization problem with the finite-l kinetic gate
included explicitly.
1. Freeze the already accepted geometry, partition functions, common basis, v12 sign, lower-order convention, JET9D8 service
and reducer.
2. Parameterize only genuinely background-null / representative-selection directions. Do not alter jets already fixed by the frozen
background equations unless they are re-solved consistently.
3. Use the full-rank Horndeski control family, not the over-restricted G4=constant subclass. Nonredundant principal targets are
(F,H,a1,c4,c2).



                                                                                                                                        Page 11


                                                                          185
    SSZ P5 / Horndeski + U(1)-SVT                                                                Full Closure Monograph 2026-09-16
SSZ P5 / Horndeski-SVT finite-l closure - technical memory - 16.09.2026


4. After selecting a new Horndeski principal member, re-solve the complete static background equations for the combined HSVT
action before evaluating finite-l stability. Do not transplant principal jets onto a background solved for another member.
5. Enforce exact background residuals and the common constraint pivots simultaneously with a finite-l lower bound K >= epsilon
I on selected L gates, beginning with L=6 and L=42.
6. Only after K>0, solve/check radial generalized eigenvalues and the angular large-L symbol. Then regenerate all 41
coefficients directly from the chosen action jets.
7. Build the action-level Outer->Central and Central->Inner matching sections; do not reuse the principal matching buffers as
proof of finite-l action continuity.
8. Freeze one global 41/41 stream and rerun the complete profile reducer. Only then open the QNM gate.
The previously suggested 00/11 inversion/rotation idea remains a useful diagnostic only where the background inverse chart
changes conditioning. The mathematically justified form is the E00, E11-E00 basis associated with kappa=-2X. It is not a
substitute for kinetic stability control.

18.3 Minimal resume sequence
         1. Read SSZ_P5_FINAL_STOP_CHECKPOINT_2026-09-16.md
         2. Read this PDF, especially Sections 2, 10, 13-17
         3. Do NOT regenerate 41/41 from scratch
         4. Do NOT revert v12 sign or a5
         5. Do NOT drop S
         6. Do NOT add reduced operators sector-by-sector
         7. Start from final_outer_variableG4_2026-09-16.py and replace only the
         representative-selection/control layer
         8. Impose K>0 at finite L as a target during the action-jet continuation
         9. Re-solve background-constrained jets
         10. Re-run full profile reducer and all acceptance gates
         11. Only after PASS: construct coupled QNM determinant


19. Literature and convention anchors
The two perturbation formalisms used throughout the coefficient work are:
          C. Zhang and R. Kase, Physical Review D 110, 044047 (2024), arXiv:2404.11910 - even-parity perturbations in U(1)-gauge-
           invariant scalar-vector-tensor theories; Appendix-A coefficients and reduced K/G/S/M structure.
          R. Kase and S. Tsujikawa, Physical Review D 107, 104045 (2023), arXiv:2301.10362 - odd/even black-hole perturbations in
           Maxwell-Horndeski theories; full five-degree-of-freedom static-spherical formalism.
Project conventions that must stay locked when comparing formulas: r_s=1; u=C=r_s/r; x=r/r_s=1/u; scalar phi=Xi; the accepted
vector numbering uses the 13-slot Zhang-Kase schema after canonicalizing the Maxwell-Horndeski v1...v9 vector block; the
selected lower-order member used in the final outer run has v5=c3=e3=0.

20. Frozen final statement
    What can safely be said as of 16 September 2026
    P5 has a regular frozen geometry, healthy local principal witnesses in the exterior, genuine-SVT light-ring lobe and core,
    constructive same-action handover existence, a complete common 41-slot unreduced coefficient language, a validated
    derivative service, complete constraint maps, validated Zhang-Kase and Maxwell-Horndeski coefficient emitters, and a
    profile-aware finite-l reducer including the antisymmetric S term. No certified global finite-l same-action member has yet been
    completed. The last variable-G4 outer candidate is rejected because sym(K) is negative and, more fundamentally, the
    changed Horndeski principal member was not followed by a fresh full background re-solve. Therefore coupled QNM remains
    blocked. Future work should formulate a targeted transverse action-jet continuation/control problem with background
    equations and finite-l K positivity imposed simultaneously.

This report intentionally records both successes and the final failure so that a later continuation does not accidentally erase hard-
won distinctions or redo already closed algebra.

  Behandlung korrigiert wurden, sind ausdrücklich als SUPERSEDED gekennzeichnet.

1. Kurzfassung und aktueller Gesamtstand
   Die P5-Arbeit ist deutlich weiter als ein bloßer lokaler Stabilitätscheck. Die geometrische P5-
Hintergrundkurve, die lokalen Horndeski- und genuine-SVT-Repräsentanten, die beiden C-infinity-Handovers,
die Hintergrund-Re-Solves, die transversalen Kontrollränge und die algebraische Eliminierung der
nondynamischen Even-Variablen sind konstruktiv geschlossen. Zusätzlich existiert ein globaler numerischer
45.617-Punkt-Export des kanonischen Principal-Operators K,R,G. [S1-S3,S11]
   Der lange offene numerische Schwachpunkt war die lower-order Massmatrix M: mehrere Einträge
enthalten radiale Ableitungen von zusammengesetzten Koeffizientenkombinationen. Post-hoc Cubic-Spline-
Ableitungen aus gerundeten CSV-Spalten erzeugten falsche M12/M23- und Randartefakte. Am 16.09. wurde
diese Ebene durch einen einheitlichen lokalen Polynomial-Jet-Service (9 Punkte, Grad 8) neu aufgebaut. Die
empfindlichen Größen v13 und e4 regressieren wieder auf den eingefrorenen Zhang-Kase-Zentralvertreter,
und die direkten finite-L-Massmatrizen konvergieren gegen alle bekannten High-L-Identitäten. [S5,S7]
   Der gegenwärtig entscheidende offene Punkt ist nicht mehr "existiert ein KRGM-Operator?", sondern die
eindeutige Vorzeichenkonvention des v12-Kanals im Übergang von der unreduzierten Action zu den
gedruckten reduzierten radialen G-Formeln. Das archivierte Appendix-A-Profil und die neue Higher-Jet-
Rekonstruktion ergeben v12=-v6/(2h). Genau diese Minusrelation wird auch vom direkten High-L-Mass-
Audit gestützt. Im aktuell weitergeführten Direct-Action-Reducer reproduziert der K-Block die publizierte
Reduktion, während der G-Block bei der Minusrelation von der gedruckten reduzierten G-Formel abweicht;
die Plusrelation reproduziert dagegen die gedruckte G-Formel. Dieser Konflikt ist noch nicht als endgültiger
Source-Fix aufgelöst. Deshalb wird in diesem Protokoll kein finaler coupled-QNM-PASS behauptet.
   Wichtigster Sicherheitsbefund
   Es wäre derzeit methodisch falsch, den vorhandenen globalen kanonischen K/R/G-Dump einfach mit einem M-
   Block aus einer anderen Vorzeichen- oder Feldbasis-Konvention zu verheiraten. Eine radiusabhängige
   Feldtransformation verändert auch lower-order Connection-/Potentialterme. Der endgültige finite-l KRGM-
   Dump muss nach Auflösung des v12-/G-Konflikts in EINER gemeinsamen physischen Feldbasis aus EINEM
   konsistenten unreduced 41-Slot-Vertreter erzeugt werden.



2. Was die Statuswörter in diesem Projekt bedeuten
   Im Verlauf der Arbeit wurden mehrere Ebenen bewusst getrennt. Diese Trennung ist entscheidend, weil
ein Existenzbeweis, ein numerischer Representative und ein Spektral-PASS unterschiedliche Aussagen sind.
